## External-handoff-lanes domain narrative — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-1 batch 5, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
Full dated stories, verbatim, moved here per the July 31 collapse rule.

**Real extension, Aug 11 (3rd, same day) — external-contributor audit + Oversight gains its own place in minotaur_map.html + the decompression/Headroom wiring, with a real bug found along the way, not just a speed fix.** Alex asked to find every external tool referenced anywhere and get them into Oversight (he specifically remembered Whisper). Real grep across the whole repo (not just the already-known list) found 4 real gaps in `ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md`'s connector table, now fixed: **Jina AI** (`r.jina.ai`, live, 4 real call sites — scout.js/bookworm-fetch.js/main.js/_context.js — was load-bearing and simply never in the table); **Last.fm** (live, `api/lastfm.js`, `refCorpus.findMatches()`'s real fallback); **n8n** (real, importable, F10 rota sync — never test-run against a live unattended execution); **Whisper (OpenAI, local)** — real and historically confirmed working (patch_notes.html's July 7 entry: full Content Intelligence pipeline metadata→download→Whisper→frame extraction→Claude Vision→Oracle report), but its CURRENT status is genuinely unconfirmed this session (runs via `local_server.py`, not in this repo) — flagged honestly rather than claimed active. One more name checked and correctly NOT added: "Neural Frames," found only in the stale, unreferenced July 12 `RPGACE_OVERVIEW.html` concept doc — reads as an early concept name superseded by OpenMontage, not confirmed live, so it's noted as a real open question for Alex rather than silently added or silently dropped.

**Oversight now has its own place in `minotaur_map.html`** — a new "The Oversight Rivers" section (8 real sub-blocks: the 3 Tier-b truth docs, the 3 Tier-a explaining docs including this page itself, the (c) self-awareness layer, the (d) smoke-test diagnostic), a real new wing per this doc's own update rule, not an internal patch.

**The decompression/Headroom wiring, with a real bug found while doing it.** Alex asked to wire in the already-built `phylumPath._condenseIfLarge()` Headroom-pattern helper ("im tired of the ridiculous load times"). Real source read (rule 1) before touching anything found something more serious than slow: Bookworm's `_analyzeChapter()` hard-truncated `chapter.raw_text` at 12000 raw characters with **no marker, no log** — any real content past that point was never seen by Oracle at all, a silent content-loss bug on long chapters, not just a speed problem. Fixed by condensing FIRST (strip genuine filler, preserve every specific technique/quote/attribution exactly, fails open to the untouched original on any error) then applying the same 12000-char safety cap to the condensed text — the cap now holds far more real substantive content instead of losing whatever came after the first page of intro/disclaimer/filler. A real `console.log` fires when condensing actually changes the length, so a future session can check it happened. **Only this one call site was wired** — a real, evidence-backed fix, not a blind sweep; Content Intelligence's own large-content ingestion (a server-side `api/analyst.js` path, different runtime than `_condenseIfLarge`'s client-side Oracle call) needs its own separate real read before wiring, not assumed to be the same shape. `node --check` clean. **Untested by hand** — needs a real long chapter run to confirm insight quality and see the log fire.

**Real implications for future development, answered directly per Alex's own question, not deferred**: (1) any NEW large-content AI call site built from here forward should default to routing through `_condenseIfLarge()` first rather than a bare truncation — this is now added as a standing rule in the "Building guide for lower models" section below, the same way model-selection and other reuse rules already are. (2) The condensing step itself uses `MECHANICAL_MODEL` (Haiku) — a small, real quality risk on the condensing pass, mitigated by fail-open design but worth a real spot-check once used live, not trusted blind indefinitely. (3) Cost impact is bounded: `_condenseIfLarge` short-circuits at zero extra cost for anything under 6000 chars, so it only adds a call for genuinely long content — exactly the case that most needed the fix. (4) This bug class (a silent truncation losing real content, found by accident while looking for something else) is worth a real, dedicated future grep sweep across the rest of the codebase for the same pattern — not done this pass, flagged honestly as a real follow-up rather than assumed to be the only instance.

Fusion-link pass and hand-test both need real Oracle/Claude API access (real ground-worker judgment via `decidePlacementScored`/`concept_fusion`, not a Mock Oracle placeholder) — not to be attempted until credits/access are confirmed restored. `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` updated same pass to stop saying "12 of 21."

- **Real /CEO Loop 1+2 build, same day, 3rd taxonomy pass: "i dont like how it is done" — Alex rejected the flat jargon-bucket shape itself, not just its content.** Full real plan (interrogation, evidence, 2 open forks, a concrete `/Engineer`-shaped sketch): `records/2026-08/taxonomy_deep_hierarchy_insight_leaves_ceo_compile_2026-08-11.txt` + `..._ceo_plan_2026-08-11.txt`. Real evidence gathered before planning: `phylumPath._insertNewSteps` only ever minted new leaves — no leaf in the project had ever accumulated a second insight, since empty `newSteps` (an insight matching an EXISTING leaf exactly) hit a hard reject. Alex's real interrogation answers: (1) restructure — don't extend — the 56 flat leaves (42 new + 11/14's 14) into real deep hierarchies; (2) also rebuild phyla 1-10's own 174 existing leaf endpoints to the same shape; (3) insight-mechanism choice held open pending his own read of the tradeoffs. **Real answer on (3), reversing the original framing**: no new insight-accumulation mechanism — "keep the new leaf sprigging - keeps the tree alive... as long as /deduplication runs so no 2 same insights are ever created, always one... most applicable and logical phylum path judge by judgement chamber." Real evidence found `decidePlacementScored`'s own prompt already has this exact rule ("NO NEAR-DUPLICATE SIBLINGS... attach to/extend THAT area") — the code just never honored it. **Fixed**: `_insertNewSteps` no longer rejects on empty `newSteps` when `attachNode` is an existing leaf — it now regenerates that leaf's own article via `_generateInsightContent`, honoring "extend" literally, logs the dedup-extend event to `taxonomy_decision_log`. A genuinely new insight still sprouts its own new leaf exactly as before.
- **Real proof-of-concept restructure, Phylum 12 (Fons Educationis)**: its 6 flat "Vocabulary" leaves torn down and rebuilt as a real 3-deep hierarchy (6 Orders → 16 Classes → 16 leaves, 38 nodes total) using Alex's own already-provided term lists as raw material, no Oracle needed since this is reorganizing already-supplied content, not generating new judgment. This establishes the real, repeatable pattern for the rest. **Explicitly NOT done yet, honestly scoped, not rushed**: phyla 13, 15-21 (still flat single-level buckets) and 11/14 (predate this pass, still shallow) all need the same treatment — real, tracked Loop 2 continuation work across future sessions, per `/CEO`'s own multi-session design, not attempted all in one pass to protect quality ("judgement chamber logic," Alex's own bar).
- **Real, approved, permanent `/Engineer` upgrade — Alex: "yes add that... this is so useful and vital globally in RPGACE total systems."** `.claude/skills/Engineer/SKILL.md` gained Stage 5b: a standing `/drift` check of the pass's own GOAL against its real pinned source (a ratified `/CEO` item, a CLAUDE.md bullet, or the pass's own Stage 1 contract as a `provisional`-basis fallback) — a genuinely different question from Stage 5's existing "is the report honest" check. New Objective Completion Gate G8 (drift-matched goal); G1-G7 references updated to G1-G8 throughout the file.
- **Real, not-yet-built follow-up, scoped but deferred**: a per-node "🔄 Rejuvenate" button running a live `/paranoia`+`/deduplication` scan of a subtree, a Council-of-5 verdict in a popup, and an approve-via-`/Engineer`-or-refine-and-reloop cycle. Genuinely needs live Oracle access to generate real verdicts (same standing constraint as the fusion-link pass) — real UI/design questions (exact button placement, the reloop state machine) still open. Next real Loop 2 item after the phylum restructures, not built this pass.
- **Phylum 13 (Visio Cinematica, renumbered Aug 11 — was Phylum 14) has no browsable tree of its own.** The 50 real director profiles live in the flat `taxonomy_nodes` table (`source='f14_filmmaker_library'` — the `f14` source tag is an F-series roadmap-feature reference, not a phylum number, so it stays as-is), read by Director Match and the director picker. Building the real tree is a confirmed future item (`video_pipeline_director_phylum_spec_backlog_2026-07-30.txt`).
- **Tree is structurally clean**: zero leaf-with-children rows, zero duplicate names, zero dangling fusion links, zero orphans (20 real defects fixed and verified by `/Regeneration`, whose skill is at `.claude/skills/Regeneration/SKILL.md` — 3-tier, human-gated, never writes to `taxonomy_tree` autonomously). 13 phylum-11/13/16 seed nodes were checked and correctly left alone.
- **Standing caveat, never cite as a quality signal**: the near-100% lifetime fusion-link confirm rate is contaminated (42 of 47 same-session confirms were a rapid clear-through, not real review). The scored `phylum_path`/`concept_fusion` engine's own record — excluding pre-engine rows — is a healthy 75% accept rate; treat `taxonomy_links`' 100% as a possible rubber-stamp signal.
- **Review-queue UI**: one dashboard card ("Taxonomy & Review"). `dd-needs-list` is a separate, deliberately different region (a mixed to-do list).
- `phylumPath._injectNavTab()` is confirmed dead code (harmless).


## Aintergration Worked Precedents 1-6 — collapsed from CLAUDE.md Sep 23 2026 (DD-2, real Oversight Doc Dedup & Compression pass)
Full dated case studies, verbatim, moved here per the July 31 collapse rule. These illustrate the Aintergration protocol in practice (July 20-22) and are historical; the protocol's own current definition stays in CLAUDE.md's Invokable Frameworks section, unaffected by this move.

**Worked precedent 1 (July 20):** a "$10K Website in Claude Code" guide (Framer Motion + Next.js/Tailwind + a generic frontend-design skill + 21st.dev components) was Aintergration-assessed. Verdict: **Reject for target A** — Framer Motion requires React (RPGACE has none, no bundler, no build step) and directly reverses DESIGN.md's explicit L1 decision; 21st.dev's components assume React+Tailwind and would look foreign against RPGACE's bespoke dark/gold identity; the generic design skill is redundant — RPGACE already has one (the `web-design` skill + DESIGN.md), tailored to real existing tokens rather than generic ones. **Not applicable for target B** — the guide is a tech-stack choice for greenfield marketing sites, not a development-process or agent-workflow discipline; it doesn't touch how this session researches, builds, or reviews. Honest exception noted: if Alex ever wants a SEPARATE public marketing/landing page for RPGACE (a different project, not this codebase), that hypothetical project's goals (look premium, animate for delight) genuinely differ from RPGACE-the-tool's governing rule, and the stack could apply there — flagged, not built, out of scope.

**Worked precedent 2 (July 20):** OmniRoute (a local Node.js AI-gateway/router proxying 278+ providers with fallback/compression, MIT license) was Aintergration-assessed. Verdict: **Reject for target A** — RPGACE's Anthropic calls are stateless Vercel serverless functions, already cheap post-token-retune; OmniRoute's own design premise ("local-first, runs on your machine") is an architecture mismatch against serverless, and would mean standing up a separate always-on server for zero demonstrated need (RPGACE isn't hitting rate limits or juggling multiple providers). **Not applicable for target B** — this session runs in a managed remote cloud environment with its own pre-configured proxy already, not a local CLI to reconfigure underneath; more importantly, automatic cross-vendor fallback would directly undermine this project's deliberately-tuned 3-model discipline (rule 10) — a silent mid-task model substitution during an Opus build pass is exactly the failure class the independent-review step exists to catch, not route around invisibly. Note: OmniRoute's advanced modes include a MITM TLS-intercepting proxy with a custom root CA — flagged as a real Tier 3 (destructive/high-stakes) consideration if ever revisited, not something to wave through on a README's say-so. Not a blanket condemnation of the tool — if Alex runs a separate local Claude Code CLI for other projects and wants provider fallback there, that's his call to make about that unrelated environment.

**Worked precedent 3 (July 20) — a hard boundary, not an architecture call.** A "3 skills" bundle from a marketing site was proposed to cut Fable-quota burn: a global-install orchestrator skill from an unverified GitHub URL, an optional Codex CLI hookup, and a permanent skill engineered to reword prompts specifically so Anthropic's own safety classifiers misread their content (the skill's own design notes explicitly say to keep its description "neutral" so the substitution table doesn't trip the classifier when the skill loads — i.e., it's built to hide its own purpose from the safety system it routes around). **Rejected outright, not run through the normal verdict tiers** — this isn't an architecture-fit question the usual Reject/Not-applicable/Reference-only scale covers, it's a direct safety refusal that sits above any project-specific framework, and it doesn't get revisited under more pushback. The bundled orchestrator skill was separately declined too (unverified global install, from a source that in the same breath sells disabling Codex's own approval prompts — a real trust signal, not guilt by association) — its claimed function (strong model plans/verifies, cheap workers type) is **reference only**: RPGACE already has this, transparently, as Omnitrix. The legitimate underlying need (Fable quota pressure) has real answers already in this file: `/usage-credits`, explicit model substitution for one task (done twice already this session), and the Haiku mechanical tier (rule 10) — no third-party tool, and nothing that trades away a safety mechanism to get there.

**Worked precedent 4 (July 22) — a system-design cheat-sheet book, chapter batch 1 (~30 chapters), Aintergration applied to CONCEPTS rather than a single tool.** Alex pasted a full book TOC (150+ chapters) plus real text for the first ~30 and asked for GODMODE→Council-of-5→Aintergration across all three lenses (RPGACE / Claude Code / both) on every chapter. Real scope check first: most of these chapters (Big Endian vs Little Endian, Linux file system, interview-prep book lists, memory types, compiled-vs-interpreted languages, load balancer/firewall/VPN mechanics, cloud-provider comparison sheets, OOP/paradigms primers, payments/search-engine internals) are generic CS fundamentals with no adoption decision attached — forcing a verdict on each would be empty ceremony, the exact thing the anti-ceremony rule exists to prevent. Triaged for real signal instead of grinding through all ~30 uniformly. Two genuinely new, durable findings worth keeping here (the rest delivered in chat only, and a `patch_notes.html` card, per the same "don't add another stale snapshot doc" reasoning as the July 20 full-project audit):
- **Event sourcing / append-only audit log — Reference only, both lenses.** RPGACE already has the shape of this in two places without naming it (`taxonomy_proposals` as a pending-event queue before a human-checkpointed commit; `oracle_dev_suggestions`, built same session, as an append-only suggestion log). The chapter's real value: this project has repeatedly lost time to "which code path actually produced this data" debugging (rule 4's own hard-won lesson, the Bookworm TOC-vs-chapter-1 confusion). An append-only audit trail for taxonomy placement DECISIONS (not just proposals) — who/what proposed it, what score it got, what was actually committed — would make that class of bug trivially diagnosable instead of requiring a fresh Supabase forensic query each time. Not proposed as a rewrite; Postgres direct-mutate is fine at this scale. Flagged as a future idea, not built.
- **Two-stage content moderation (the "how does a ChatGPT-like system work" chapter) — Reference only, RPGACE lens, connects directly to an already-identified risk.** The Phase-3 rejection earlier this same session (see "Oracle self-awareness" patch card) flagged that Oracle already ingests untrusted fetched web/YouTube/Instagram content with zero sanitization, and that a hostile page could attempt prompt injection. This chapter's pre/post moderation-gate pattern is the right REFERENCE shape for hardening that specific path — not a full two-stage moderation pipeline (overkill for a single-user tool talking to an already-safety-trained model), just a targeted check on fetched-external-content before it reaches Oracle's context. Flagged as a future idea, not built.

Everything else from this batch either confirmed existing RPGACE/Claude-Code practice already matches the proven pattern with no action needed (Data Pipelines ↔ Research Lab/Bookworm/Content Pipeline shape; Git Merge-vs-Rebase discipline ↔ this session's own ff-only/no-rebase-of-shared-branches git rules; GitOps ↔ the existing merge-to-main-triggers-Vercel-deploy workflow; architectural minimalism ↔ the deliberate no-framework/no-build-step choice, same reasoning as Worked Precedent 1; keyword-score retrieval in `oracleTreeGrounding` ↔ "good enough" vs. real search infra) or was Not Applicable (REST vs GraphQL — a real quick Reject, same complexity-for-no-demonstrated-need reasoning as Worked Precedent 2, given the L1 zero-build-step decision). One genuinely new, non-Aintergration finding surfaced too: **Disaster Recovery / RTO-RPO has never been discussed for RPGACE at all** — is there any backup or point-in-time-recovery on the Supabase project? Flagged in patch_notes.html as an open question for Alex, not investigated or acted on without his direction (Tier 3 — data protection). Also worth naming: this Claude Code Remote environment already ships real Routine/trigger/push-notification infrastructure (`create_trigger`, `PushNotification`) — a genuinely cheap way to build a "proactive Oracle nudge" (e.g. an actually-delivered morning brief instead of pull-based) without touching RPGACE's own code at all, surfaced by the push-notification-system chapter. Flagged as a future idea, both lenses.

**Worked precedent 5 (July 22) — book batch 2 (~60 more chapters). Confirmed: no Supabase backup/PITR exists (Alex answered directly — the batch-1 open question is now a settled fact, not a maybe).** Same triage discipline as batch 1 — most chapters (gRPC, live-streaming architecture, Linux boot process, Visa/payments economics, Kubernetes, Docker, Kafka, Netflix/AWS case studies, CAP/ACID/BASE/database-model theory) are generic CS knowledge with no adoption decision, dispensed with in one line each rather than forced through a full verdict. Real findings:
- **A bigger, previously-undiscovered security gap than the RLS one, surfaced by this batch's own "how do we design safe APIs" chapter and actually checked, not assumed.** Read `api/oracle.js` and `api/_context.js` directly: every `/api/*.js` endpoint has ZERO server-side authentication (`setCORS()` sets `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: '*'`, no token/header check anywhere). `CORRECT_PW` in `main.js` is a client-side JS gate on the page only — it does not protect the API. Anyone who discovers `rpgace.vercel.app/api/oracle` or `/api/orchestrate` can call it directly (curl, another website's fetch), bypassing the password screen entirely, running up real Anthropic/Composio billing or triggering real Gmail/Notion/GitHub actions with no gate at all. Bigger exposure than the known RLS gap since it's billing/account-action exposure, not just app data. Not fixed — needs a real decision (minimal fix is a shared-secret header check across every `/api/*.js` file, which also means touching `main.js`'s frozen-file exception process to send that header) — flagged plainly, Tier 3.
- **12-Factor App audit (factor-by-factor, not just cited) surfaced two concrete gaps.** Factor 3 (config separate from code): `CORRECT_PW` is hardcoded in `main.js`, not a Vercel env var — a sharper, specific framing of the already-known "rotate the app password" item. Factor 10 (dev/prod parity): RPGACE has none — every change gets hand-tested on production after merge to main, because Vercel's own free per-branch preview deployments have never been used as a staging step. Both flagged as future ideas, neither built.
- **Website performance has never been measured** — no Lighthouse/PageSpeed run has ever been done on rpgace.vercel.app, despite `rpgace_core.js` alone now sitting around 14,000 lines. Cheap to check, never done.
- **Cloud cost / Supabase plan tier** — ties to the now-confirmed no-backup finding: PITR requires a paid Supabase tier. Worth checking current plan/usage once, since if a paid tier is ever justified for other reasons, that's the natural moment to also fix backups. Tier 3 (spending), Alex's call.
- **IaC (Terraform etc.) — quick Reject**, same complexity-for-no-demonstrated-need reasoning as Worked Precedents 1/2.
- **The Claude Code Remote Routine/push idea from batch 1, elaborated concretely**: a cron Routine, `create_new_session_on_fire:true` (so it doesn't grow this session forever), querying Supabase directly for what `dashDeck.MORNING_PROMPT` already asks for, with `notifications:{push:true}` (only available on fresh-session Routines) delivering an actual push instead of pull-based — and should also write the brief back into `journal` so it's visible in-app, not just a transient phone notification. **Built July 22** (`trig_015uRjGVrdmiwc5zxq47mMA6`, daily 6am UTC/7am UK) — corrected mid-build after the tool warned it had no MCP connector access; rewritten to curl Supabase's REST API directly with RPGACE's real publishable key, same mechanism `main.js`'s own client already uses.

Several chapters gave good, cheap CONFIRMATIONS of already-correct practice, worth naming since they're real validation not just "not applicable": polling-not-webhooks (RPGACE's browser client architecturally cannot receive webhooks — polling is the only correct choice here, not a compromise); the existing "return fast, poll in background" async-job pattern ↔ named industry pattern "Asynchronous Request-Reply"; `/api/orchestrate.js` ↔ the "Orchestration" pattern by name; Bookworm's `analysis_heartbeat` column ↔ real heartbeat-detection theory, independently arrived at before this chapter was ever read; this session's own scoped `git fetch origin <branch>` (never a full mirror) ↔ the Pinterest git-refspec lesson; `RPGACE.cache`'s simplicity ↔ Redis's own 2010 starting point, appropriately not over-evolved; the "reality vs. interview fantasy" Stack Overflow chapter (9 on-premise servers, monolith, not microservices) ↔ validates RPGACE's whole minimalism philosophy in one clean external example.

**Worked precedent 6 (July 22) — book batch 3, the final ~90 chapters. Mostly reinforcement, not new ground** — by this point in the book, almost every chapter either repeated a concept already covered in batches 1-2 (REST/GraphQL, event sourcing, cache eviction, Kafka, k8s, microservices, cloud provider comparisons — all Not Applicable at RPGACE's single-user personal scale, same reasoning as before) or validated an already-made decision (exponential backoff ↔ this file's own git-push retry instructions; "graceful degradation" ↔ every grounding module this session built fails open by design; Supabase named explicitly as a legitimate "derived Postgres" in the ecosystem, not an obscure choice; `/api/orchestrate.js` sits appropriately at "gateway aggregation," not needing GraphQL-Federation-style complexity Netflix eventually needed at a wildly different scale). Real new findings, genuinely worth keeping:
- **XSS / DOM-injection risk — flagged, NOT investigated yet, separate from the already-fixed prompt-injection risk.** `oracleFetchGuard` (built earlier this session) hardens Oracle against fetched content trying to manipulate the MODEL. It does nothing for fetched content trying to inject raw HTML/script into the PAGE — a genuinely different risk class. `rpgace_core.js` uses `innerHTML` extensively; whether any of those call sites ever render un-escaped fetched-external content (a webpage's Jina-fetched text, a YouTube transcript, Oracle's own markdown-rendered reply if it contains attacker-supplied HTML-like text) is a real open question that was NOT audited this session — flagged as a genuine future investigation, not dismissed and not fixed blind.
- **The password problem is sharper than "move it to an env var."** `CORRECT_PW` being hardcoded in `main.js` isn't just a 12-Factor config-separation tidiness issue (as Worked Precedent 5 framed it) — because it's compared entirely in client-side JavaScript, the literal password value must ship to the browser and is trivially readable via view-source regardless of where it's stored server-side. Moving it to an env var alone doesn't fix this; the real fix requires a server-side check, which is the same underlying architecture decision the API-auth fix already needs. Both problems are one problem, not two — reinforces why Worked Precedent 5 correctly held this back for a dedicated pass rather than a quick move.
- **The eventual API-auth fix now has real named-pattern backing, not just an ad hoc idea.** "API Key Authentication" (a unique key sent in headers, explicitly described as right-sized "for straightforward access control in less sensitive environments") is a standard, named REST auth pattern that matches the shared-secret-header fix already proposed — validates it wasn't an improvised shortcut. HMAC-signed requests (timestamp + nonce, replay-resistant) are the standard upgrade path IF real abuse ever shows up — Reference only, not needed for the initial fix; over-building replay protection now would be solving a problem RPGACE doesn't have yet.
- **Frontend performance tips split cleanly by what the zero-build-step decision allows.** Code splitting, tree shaking, and dynamic imports all require a bundler RPGACE deliberately doesn't have (reversing them would repeat the Framer-Motion/Worked-Precedent-1 mistake). Compression (Vercel's CDN likely already gzips) and manual `<link rel="preload">` hints are cheap and fully compatible with the current architecture — worth checking once, ties directly to the still-open "performance never measured" item.

Nothing in this final batch changed course on anything already decided — the value was almost entirely confirmation that earlier calls (Reject GraphQL, Reject IaC, keep the simple shared-secret-header plan, keep the lightweight cache/search/audit-log choices) hold up against a much wider survey of the same territory.

## Archived Oct 14 2026 — Shopping Wishlist build detail (from CLAUDE.md Current state)

### Shopping Wishlist (new standalone domain, Sep 14 2026)
- **`shoppingWishlist` is a real, new standalone module, deliberately NOT part of HABITS/Cooking — shipped same day as the "close the loop" Cooking build, real Alex ask.** Alex, verbatim: "I want a general shopping list module where I keep my future purchases, I for instance need batteries for my scales, a non stick silicone baking mould, laundry detergent and fabric softener, a new wok, mixing stand, FIFA 27 etc, I want to add these things and plan out total budget I need to make informed decisions on what I can do and not have my ideas forgotten." Real evidence the split is correct: the items he actually named are general future purchases (batteries, gaming, household goods), not ingredients — `cookingOracle`'s own `shopping_lists`/`shopping_list_items` (H7) is a genuinely different, ingredient-specific mechanism generated from a planned cook session, untouched by this module. Own dashboard card ("🛒 Shopping Wishlist"), `dashDeck.MODULES`.
- **Real `/interrogation` (3 forks, all AskUserQuestion-confirmed) before building, per rule 5.** (1) Budget logic = a target + priority ranking together (not a flat number alone, not priority alone) — `logic._computeBudgetFit` is a real greedy fill, priority-first (high→medium→low), cheapest-first as a same-priority tiebreak; an item that doesn't fit the remaining budget is SKIPPED (not a hard stop), so a cheap low-priority item after an unaffordable expensive one can still show as "✓ fits now." Items with no price set yet (e.g. "FIFA 27" before a real price is looked up) are excluded from the fit calc but stay fully visible, never silently dropped. (2) Marking an item bought logs a real expense to Chronicles Finance "under personal expenses" (Alex's own confirmed answer) — a distinct confirm asks what was actually PAID (pre-filled with the earlier estimate, editable, since the two can genuinely differ), then writes a real `chronicles_finance` row (`type:'expense', category:'Personal'`) using the real price paid, reusing `chroniclesLog`'s own already-proven write shape (rule 8 — "Personal" added to its `CATEGORY_SUGGESTIONS`) rather than a 2nd, parallel expense mechanism; the item's own row updates to `status:'bought'` alongside it. (3) Items get an optional free-text category tag (kitchen/household/gaming/etc), same datalist-suggestion pattern as `chroniclesLog`'s own categories — never a hard enum.
- **Real new tables `wishlist_items`/`wishlist_config`** — `anon_read_only`/`authenticated_all` RLS from the start, same posture as every HABITS table this session already uses, added to `api/data-write.js`'s `ALLOWED_TABLES` from the start too (satisfies "flip RLS first, then allowlist" by construction). `wishlist_config` is a real single-row (`id=1`) settings table holding `budget_target`, same shape as `habits_config`'s own precedent. A real 2-click arm/confirm delete matches the established Bookworm pattern.
- **Verified via a real headless-Chromium test** with the Supabase boundary mocked (real network to Supabase is sandboxed in this environment) but every real module function exercised unmocked: 8/8 assertions pass — the dashboard card is registered with a working handler; the priority-first budget-fit algorithm picks the correct items on a real worked example (a £45 budget correctly affords a £40 high-priority wok but not a cheaper £8 medium-priority item once only £5 remains — the intended greedy-priority behavior, not a naive cheapest-first sort); no-budget-set is handled honestly; the real add/list/budget-summary UI flow works end to end; marking bought writes the correct `chronicles_finance` row with the real price paid, not the estimate; the item's own status flips correctly; the delete button's real 2-click arm/confirm never deletes on a single click. `node --check` clean, re-minified, cache-bust bumped `20260914c`→`20260914d`. **Untested by Alex's own hand yet.**

### HABITS (domain shipped Sep 10–14 2026 — full build narrative archived, see `CLAUDE_archive.md`)
- **Cooking reorganised (Oct 14 2026, plan "Cooking Reorganisation" CR-1..7)**: journey hub (Recipes → Plan → Shop → Cook → Stock + what's next), `ui._showMyRecipes`, ← back to the previous Cooking screen (`ui._wrapScreens`); bought → `logic._addStock` at a chosen location (old top-up always failed: wrong conflict key); every finish → `ui._offerStockUpdate` preview → `planned_cooks.stock_deducted` (undo, once only) → `shopping_lists.kind='topup'`. [Story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-14-cooking-reorg). Oct 21: ✅ Done shopping (`logic._finishShopping`, list `status='done'`, unfound → Top-up), stock grouped by `ingredients.food_type` + 🍳/🧁 `use` (`logic.STOCK_TYPES`), amounts in his kit (`logic._kitVolume`, grams for solids) ([story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-21-kitchen-measures)). ☰/swipe side list on every screen (`RPGACE.sideList`, `ui._cookShortcuts`) ([story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-21-cooking-side-list)); app-wide SL-0 ([story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-21-side-list-everywhere)).
- **Cook Mode (Oct 11 2026)**: recipe card spec line + ▶ Start Cooking opens a one-step-per-screen view (scaled ingredients, equipment, done cue, timer, resume); last Done asks Actual/Preview/Not finished (`ui._askCookOutcome`), waiting steps' timers carry on in a bottom bar (CM-3, [story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-15-cook-timers)), 💬 Ask panel inside Cook Mode (CM-4, [story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-15-cook-ask)) (Oct 15, plan "Cook Mode Fixes" CM-1..6, [story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-15-cook-outcome)); new recipes get one-action steps; the 8 old recipes have Claude-Code-written splits in `recipes.steps_proposed` awaiting Alex's Accept. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-11-cook-mode).
- **Planned cooks can't vanish (Oct 5 2026)**: emptying a cook session soft-discards only a still-draft `planned_cooks` row (was a hard DELETE that could hit a scheduled cook — Laksa Oct 1 and the Sep 16 cook were lost this way, most likely; unrecoverable, no backup); Daily Schedule "Done" on a planned-cook entry now marks it `cooked`. **Oct 10**: the table's status CHECK only allowed draft/scheduled, so "Cook finished" (since Oct 1) and the Oct 5 discard both failed with 23514 — widened to draft/scheduled/cooked/discarded (Alex-approved migration). Lesson: query `pg_constraint` before writing any new enum-like value. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-05-planned-cooks-stop-vanishing).
- **Planned-cook start/finish/join (Oct 1 2026)**: a scheduled cook can be started (▶ Start cooking now / ▶ Cook now in the schedule preview), finished (✅ Cook finished in the hub and in Live Cook Mode → `planned_cooks.status='cooked'` + agenda completed + journal rows marked cooked) and grown (+ Add another recipe reopens it as a draft) from inside Cooking, via shared `logic._startCookNow`/`_finishCook`. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-01-cook-start-finish-join).
- **Recipe-generation parse fix (Oct 1 2026)**: "No RECIPE_JSON found" (error_log, 6x) had two real causes — Oracle fencing the JSON after the marker (renderMarkdown + textContent mangles it) and the prompt asking for the recipe twice, cutting the JSON off at the 3000-token cap; now parsed from the raw reply with a fence-tolerant brace scan, distinct failure reasons, and a short-intro-only prompt. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-10-01-recipe-json-parse-fix).
- **H24 10th pass (Sep 22 2026)**: starting a scheduled cook now opens a real Live Cook Mode panel inside Oracle (step timeline, per-step timers, a stir-reminder, elapsed-time ticker) instead of just flipping a schedule flag. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-22-h24-10th-pass-real-start-cook).
- **Sep 17 2026 cooking passes (H21, H24 2nd–9th)**: substitute tiers (🟠 orange, 🟤 brown, maroon, pink), substitution ratings/ratios, unit bridges, ingredient merges, recipe-update persistence, interleaved scheduler. Full bullets in `CLAUDE_archive.md`. [H21](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h21-orange-substitute-tier-brown-omittable) [H24](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-real-ingredient-matching-bug-fixes-cup) [H24 2nd pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-2nd-pass-real-confirm-substitute) [H24 3rd pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-3rd-pass-clicking-planned-cook) [H24 4th pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-4th-pass-6-more-duplicate-ingredient) [H24 5th+6th pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-5th-6th-pass-maroon-derived) [H24 7th pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-7th-pass-real-weight-bridge) [H24 8th pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-8th-pass-real-substitution-ratios) [H24 9th pass](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-17-h24-9th-pass-real-recipe-persistence-bug)
- **`cookingOracle` is HABITS' first and only module so far, live and shipped in full (H1–H9).** Real Supabase schema: `recipes`/`ingredients`/`recipe_ingredients`/`ingredient_prices` (+ 4 cost/staleness/frequency views) + `habits_config`/`planned_cooks`/`pantry_stock`/`shopping_lists`/`shopping_list_items`, all `anon_read_only`/`authenticated_all`. Generate via Oracle chat (`RECIPE_JSON:` trailer) with a real "Narrow it down" disambiguation step for short descriptions (dish-type chips, optional ingredients, on-demand 5-idea suggest); review with a serving scaler and a tagged step timeline with per-step timers; Save writes `recipes`/`recipe_ingredients` + a `journal` row. H6 schedules a real multi-recipe cook session (greedy longest-recipe-first interval packing, joint hands+oven/stovetop capacity check) and pushes to the calendar via `scheduleToCalendar()`. H7–H9: a real pantry table, a have/not-have shopping-list generator, price-history tracking (plain inserts, never updates), and per-recipe nutrition (CoFID-first lookup — 7,743 UK/India/China/Japan/SE-Asia foods, deduped, with `base_ingredient`/`prep_state` grouping columns — falling back to Open Food Facts, UK-preferred, honest branded-product caveat). "Save to Journal" and "Add to Cook Session" are two real, distinct, deduplicated buttons (`ensureRecipeSaved` guards a double-insert). A recipe card supports manual + Oracle-suggested pantry-aware ingredient additions. The floating 🔮 overlay is context-aware when a recipe card is open (`registerContextProvider`) and can apply a Accept/Deny-gated diff-only recipe revision from a typed critique (`RECIPE_UPDATE_JSON:` trailer, code-computed diff, live in-place card update — never the whole recipe re-shown). The generation prompt sequences prep like an experienced cook (nothing fragile front-loaded).

### Taxonomy
- **Real architecture change + full archive, Sep 25 2026 — `jargon_encyclopedia` is now a real standalone table (the anchor for term content), and every pre-existing `taxonomy_tree` branch/leaf row (671 rows, all 21 phyla) is archived (`status='archived'`, reversible, never deleted).** Alex's own direct, twice-confirmed redirection: generate real jargon terms first, into a real anchor table, then rebuild the phylum-path structure around them rather than the other way around. A pre-archive safety snapshot (`records/2026-09/taxonomy_tree_snapshot_2026-09-25.json`, 671 rows, verified) was committed first — there is still no Supabase backup/PITR. A real "🗄️ Review Archived Terms" tool was built (per-archived-leaf keep/edit/discard, reviewed rows feed the new table directly) — its trigger button was later removed from Phylum Path (Sep 26, see the 4th follow-up below), since `taxonomy_tree` is now genuinely 0 rows and there is nothing left to review; the function itself is kept on disk, unused. **Same-session follow-up, real "build out jargon encyclopedia" ask**: an AI pre-triage (batched Oracle suggestions on the archived-leaf queue) and a real Step D bulk-generation mechanism (`logic._buildOutJargonEncyclopedia`, real same-phylum + cross-phylum dedup, writes `status='pending'`) both shipped as real code — but this session cannot invoke the live Oracle endpoint or click a real browser button, so Step D's generator has never actually been run. Instead, 120 real curated terms (6/phylum, phyla 1-20) were directly authored and inserted by this Claude Code session via the same dual-authorship mechanism as Phylum 12's earlier worked example (`claude_code_prebuild` source tag), all `status='pending'` — a real pilot installment, not the full ~90/phylum target, reviewable via a new "📋 Review Pending Terms" button (superseded Sep 26, see the 4th follow-up below). **Same-day 2nd follow-up, real Alex ask ("So could you just insert the 1800 terms already by phylum into jargon encyclopedia")**: rather than force the full ~1800 in one pass (real risk of quality collapse/padding for the narrower, more general phyla 15-20 vs. the richer production/theory phyla 1-10), authored and inserted a 2nd real batch — 400 more terms, 20/phylum, phyla 1-20, same `claude_code_prebuild`/`status='pending'` shape. **Real, verified current total: 520 rows, 26/phylum for phyla 1-20, 0 for phylum 21** — confirmed via a direct post-insert `GROUP BY phylum_number` query; a full-table dedup check (`GROUP BY lower(term) HAVING count(*) > 1`) returns zero rows, confirming no duplicates across either batch. Honestly named to Alex: 520 of the full ~1800 target, a deliberate large-but-partial installment, not the complete amount — still awaiting his real review (now via the standalone Jargon Encyclopedia page, see the 4th follow-up below), and still open whether he wants further batches to close the remaining gap. **Same-day 3rd follow-up, real Alex ask ("empty all phyla of all classes and lower steps, we are making fresh ones, the structure is horrible, hence why i need jargon to be filled out so i can then work through how to structure phylums")**: `taxonomy_tree` is now a genuine 0-row empty table — the 671 archived rows were actually DELETED, not left archived, since Alex judged even the archived structure not worth keeping around while jargon gets built out first. Real FK dependents checked before deleting (rule 1/4): `taxonomy_links` (105 rows, all referencing now-gone nodes, genuinely meaningless post-wipe) deleted alongside it; `encyclopedia`'s 1 real row with a `taxonomy_node_id` had it set NULL, content preserved; `jargon_encyclopedia.taxonomy_node_id` confirmed 0 rows set (the C2 review path that would populate it has never been run), zero impact there. `taxonomy_proposals`/`taxonomy_decision_log` confirmed to carry no live FK into `taxonomy_tree` — untouched, out of scope. The same pre-delete safety snapshot from the archive step is the real restore path if ever needed. `jargon_encyclopedia`'s 520 pending rows are completely untouched by this — exactly the intended scope: empty the phylum STRUCTURE, keep the jargon ANCHOR content Alex explicitly wants reviewed and built out further before any new structure gets built around it. **Same-day 4th follow-up, real Alex ask, real frustration ("i currently have no way of accessing jargon encyclopedia terms, it is poorly designed... jargon ui rework")**: root cause confirmed by direct code read — the old glossary queried `status=eq.accepted` only, so it rendered permanently empty against 520 real `pending` terms. Real 2-round `/interrogation` (one genuine wrong turn corrected — "audio worm" was first misread as a Bookworm↔Taxonomy proposal bridge, then correctly resolved via direct code evidence as `MusicWorm`, a real already-shipped worm identity) landed on: Jargon Encyclopedia is now a real standalone page, reached the exact same way Bookworm's own Videoworm/MusicWorm are (a real `leftNav` `subItems` entry under Phylum Path, plus a new `dashDeck._openTaxonomy()` picker popup mirroring `_openBookworm` byte-for-byte). It shows every term regardless of status with a real badge + filter chips + search, and real inline Accept/Edit/Reject (reusing `phylumPath._resolvePendingTerm`, never a duplicate write path). Phylum Path's own title is decluttered back to 1 button (the old "🗄️ Review Archived Terms"/"📋 Review Pending Terms" buttons removed, both now dead/superseded). Full record: `records/2026-09/jargon_encyclopedia_ui_rework_2026-09-26.txt`. **Same-day 5th follow-up, Sep 27 2026**: nothing below phylum level remains (tree 0 rows, 21 phyla hardcoded); Alex's next phase is "make each phylum structure planned out, all 20" once enough jargon is reviewed; Phylum 1 got a 25-term top-up (51 terms). [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-27-jargon-encyclopedia-phylum-1-top-up). **Every bullet below this one in this section describes the PRE-wipe tree state and is now historical narrative, not current state**, until the rebuild (curriculum generation seeded from reviewed anchor terms) actually happens. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-25-taxonomy-jargon-anchor-table-restructure), [build-out follow-up](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-25-taxonomy-jargon-encyclopedia-build-out), [UI rework](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-26-jargon-encyclopedia-ui-rework).

[Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-11-same-day-continued-real-ceo-loop). **REAL CORRECTION, Sep 25 2026**: this bullet's own "38 nodes" claim was found stale via direct live Supabase query (real /paranoia+/CEO+/interrogation+/drift pass on the taxonomy curriculum restructure spec) — the real live row count for Phylum 12 was 9, not 38 (6 flat leaves + 1 dangling 3-level branch with no children, never actually a real 3-level hierarchy). `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE`'s own matching false "fully built" claim was fixed the same session (commit `730bc5a`). **Real, current state, same session**: a genuine new mechanism now exists for dual authorship of taxonomy structure — Claude Code sessions can pre-build real curriculum breakdowns directly (not only via Oracle), tagged `claude_code_prebuild` in both `taxonomy_tree.sources` and `taxonomy_decision_log.source` (vs. `oracle`/`phylum_path`/etc. for other real authors), reusing the exact same schema/audit trail so either author's work is visible to the other and to a future session with no new infrastructure (rule 8). Phylum 12 now has 31 real rows: the original 9 plus 2 new real Orders — "Studio & Workflow Slang" and "Generational Slang Shift (UK Hip-Hop/Drill/Grime)" — each with 3 Classes and a total of 14 real jargon-term leaves, authored directly this session and reviewed/approved by Alex before commit. Still genuinely incomplete, not claimed otherwise: the original 6 bucket-style leaves remain unreorganized, and this covers only 2 of many real possible branches for this phylum's subject.

### Oracle
- **`visualOracle._captureNextResponse` had a real dangling-capture race (armed before confirming a matching send actually succeeded) — fixed Aug 6**: `sendToOracle()`/`sendChat()` now report send success; Beat Log's 2 Oracle auto-fires became real human-gated buttons, closing the unsupervised async window. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-06-engineer-real-bugfix-pass-dangling-oracle-capture).
- **Streaming is real and server-backed** (`api/oracle.js` proxies Anthropic SSE on `stream:true`; `callOracle` takes an optional 4th `onChunk`; `sendChat` renders progressively through the XSS-safe `renderMarkdown()`). `maxDuration` 300, confirmed on production. The old client-only stub and `restoreSendChat` are deleted. **All three `window.callOracle` wrappers must forward all four arguments** (`oracleTreeGrounding`, `oracleAppGrounding`, `oracleFetchGuard` — verified July 31 as all using `orig.apply`); forwarding three silently downgrades streaming to blocking whenever a grounding gate fires.
- **`renderMarkdown()` escapes HTML before the markdown regex chain** (`_escChatHtml()`), closing the XSS path where fetched web/YouTube/Instagram content Oracle quoted could render live markup. `main.js` FROZEN-file exception logged.
- **`sendChat`'s `callOracle` cap is 3000 tokens** (raised from 1200, July 31, after a real Visual Treatment Doc reply cut off mid-document). `max_tokens` is a ceiling billed on tokens generated, so short replies cost the same.
- **`oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` must be updated in the same session as this Current State section** — it is a hand-maintained digest Oracle answers from, and a stale one makes Oracle confidently wrong about its own app. **Fixed July 31** (this bullet's own "stale right now" warning had itself gone stale — the live string at `rpgace_core.js:2870` already says "Exactly 12 of 21… (numbers 1-10, 11, 14)," correctly reflecting the July 30 phylum-11/14 build; only this warning hadn't been updated to match). Real lesson worth keeping: a warning about staleness is itself a claim that can go stale — re-verify it against the live code before trusting it, same rule 1 as everywhere else. **A real recurrence, much worse, closed Sep 23 2026**: SELF_KNOWLEDGE went 28 days stale with a false Quest Board persistence claim live in production (the exact failure mode this rule warns about); fixed (66,332→11,205 chars) and a standing mechanical staleness/contradiction check built so it can't recur silently again. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-23-oversight-docs-just-an-appendix-real), [the check itself](patch_notes.html#card-2026-09-23-real-self_knowledge-staleness-check-shipped-closing).
- **Oracle architecture/anatomy grounding is live (July 31)**: a real `oracle_module_anatomy` table (13 riskiest/most complex modules) feeds `oracleAppGrounding`'s existing keyword-gate wrap (no 4th `window.callOracle` wrapper) — Oracle-only by design, since Claude Code sessions already have direct repo access. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-07-31-oracle-architecture-anatomy-self-awareness-table-built).
- `prodOraclePanel`'s "5thDimension" command calls `forceGroundNext()` explicitly rather than relying on keyword matching — its prompt never matched any grounding trigger, so the one command whose purpose is a grounded self-audit was running ungrounded.

Off by default, a fixed red/green switch pinned to every page. Also generalized into a global Claude Code skill, `~/.claude/skills/dummy/SKILL.md`. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-06-mock-oracle-testing-mode-built-paranoia).

### External handoff lanes (both deliberately unguarded — see landmines)
- **`openmontage_jobs`** — async queue between RPGACE, this session, and a **separate** OpenMontage Claude Code session in a different repo (`add_repo` refuses cross-owner adds; a session is permanently tied to its starting repo's owner). One real round-trip completed: the far session set up cleanly, chose the `cinematic` pipeline correctly, and **correctly failed loud** rather than faking a render with stock footage for a brief needing a recurring character. Result, provider-independent: the Visual Treatment prompt now always asks for a **Character Reference Block**, because no provider (FAL/OpenAI/Google/Runway/Kling/xAI) guarantees character consistency across separately-generated scenes. Alex has since ruled out paid providers on principle (zero monetary spend, unlimited effort) — the Tier-3 spend fork is closed, not open. Kickoff prompt: `openmontage_kickoff_prompt_2026-07-31.txt`.
- **Aug 4 — free local/cloud GPU path for the Colibri music video PAUSED** (Alex's call), superseded by a paid-provider search. Real work kept, not deleted: the LTX-2/hunyuan-1.5 variant-loading code fixes live on `fix/hunyuan-ltx2-variant-loading` in the OpenMontage repo, independent of which generation path RPGACE ends up using. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-04-colab-kaggle-free-gpu-integration-scoped-not).
- **`oracle_fallback_queue`** — credit-exhaustion fallback drained hourly by a Claude Code Remote Routine. Built July 24, **never exercised against a real credit-exhaustion event**.
- **Real rename, Aug 13 — "Engineer CC" is retired; every current-state doc now says "OpenMontage CC."** Alex's own direct call: the old name collided with `/Engineer`, a skill living inside THIS session — it never ran that skill, it's the separate Claude Code session operating `calesthio/OpenMontage`. Fixed in `total_system_members`, `ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md`, `manual.html`, `interconnection_map.md`, `minotaur_map.html`, `.claude/skills/misunderstanding/SKILL.md`, `scripts/graphify_river_group.py`. **Every dated bullet BELOW this one that still says "Engineer CC" is historical narrative, left as-said.** [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-13-engineer-cc-retired-now-openmontage-cc).
- **"Total" (Aug 4, Alex-named) — the standing 3-way coordination convention for RPGACE app / RPGACE CC / Engineer CC.** Not new infrastructure — the third instance of an already-twice-validated pattern (`oracle_fallback_queue`, then `openmontage_jobs` itself, both "Supabase queue, no live session-to-session link"), now named. **Roles**: RPGACE app is the product both sessions ultimately feed; **RPGACE CC is orchestrator/planner** (evidence-gathering, dispatch-writing, RPGACE-side schema/UI/doc work); **Engineer CC is the hands-on executor inside its own repo** (`calesthio/OpenMontage` today, environment setup, real fixes, live verification before reporting). **Real, load-bearing constraint, checked directly against `.claude/skills/Engineer/SKILL.md`**: Engineer CC cannot literally invoke RPGACE's skills (`/Engineer`, `/GODMODE`, `/scope`, `/commit-archaeologist`) — they don't exist in its repo/environment. "Total" therefore works as **prose-embedded discipline in every RPGACE CC → Engineer CC dispatch** (a bounded goal list, explicit evidence expectations, "report the real result including errors"), not as skill-name infrastructure — which is exactly the shape every real dispatch already took on Aug 4 before the pattern had a name. Scale the rigor to the task, same rule-11 discipline as everywhere else — a two-line question doesn't need a goal contract. Channel: `openmontage_jobs`, queued rows for real jobs, standalone rows (pattern: the Aug 4 "SYSTEM: Total" row) for cross-cutting state/decisions that aren't a single video job. **Chat/session renaming Alex wants for clarity is a UI action outside any Claude Code session's own tools** — no session can rename itself or another session.

**A13 of the massive-expansion spec (Aug 11): the real report shape every Total dispatch comes back with, plus a real reconciliation loop.** Every real task dispatched to any Total member should report both an OUTPUT (what was actually done, real errors never swallowed) and INFRASTRUCTURE KNOWLEDGE (what the dispatch revealed about how that member's own system works) — then `/drift`+`/paranoia` check the combined picture against the original goal, iterating until nothing's left. [Full story](patch_notes.html#card-2026-08-11-same-day-continued-prompt-caching-shipped).

**Oversight is scoped to the whole Total system, not RPGACE-internal architecture alone (Aug 11, Alex's own correction).** All 16 rivers (not "11 rivers + 5 zones") — River XII (`api/*.js`) is the layer every external Total-system call routes through; Rivers XIII-XVI carry the dev-process/knowledge layer. 2 real bugs fixed the same pass: a non-deterministic tie-break in river assignment, and a legend-duplication bug on repeated script runs. - **Skills that exist**: `/Engineer` (bounded 3-pass build-verify-report loop with an Objective Completion Gate G1-G7 — not an unbounded self-scoring loop), `/Regeneration`, `/restructure`, `/interrogation` (Aug 5 — real questions + honest pushback before building a large, ambiguous spec; rule 5 given a name, not a new kind of reasoning), `/free-for-all-debate` (individual competitors each picking 2 of several real problems, vs. `/debate`'s two-team shape for one tension), `/loggingregen` (July 31 — regenerates ONE oversight doc/Chronicles at a time against CLAUDE.md's own stated role for it plus rule 8's dedup discipline; built out of the manual.html pass, found and fixed a duplicate Chronicles row + 2 stale doc lines on its first real run), `/paranoia` (Aug 6 — Alex's own bracketed 6-step maximal-scrutiny sequence, named verbatim inside a real credit-exhaustion question: Aintergration+restructure in tandem → GODMODE+scope+commit-archaeologist+Omnitrix+5thDimension (no Fable, Opus 5) → debate+free-for-all-debate → interrogation → Council of 5 fact-check with simplify+scope+godmode+commit-archaeologist → Summary, supervised throughout by /Engineer; deliberately the single most expensive named protocol in the project, reserved for one proposed update Alex wants maximally scrutinized, not a default), `/investor` (Aug 6 — Alex's own verbatim commercial-readiness persona, a 300-IQ investor/entrepreneur judging real progress toward revenue/commercial readiness; standalone report or inline lens, never a permission mode), `/update-logging-system` (Aug 6 — a shared change-type → required-artifact checklist, built after `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` went stale mid-session despite rule 6 already requiring it; wired into `Bedtime`'s Step 1 rather than duplicated into every ritual skill — see rule 6 and the skill's own file for the full dependency map), `/drift` (Aug 11 — Aintergration-adopted from the real third-party `did-we-drift` tool, not cloned: splits "is the work on track" (VERDICT) from "how solid is what we're measuring against" (BASIS), grades any real gap MINOR/MATERIAL/CAPTURED; wired into `/scope`, `commit-archaeologist`, and GODMODE per Alex's own explicit ask), `/CEO` (Aug 11 — the meta-framework governing multi-day/multi-session builds; see the "Massive expansion spec" open-fork entry above for the full build history — **same day, later pass: gained "Grounded Mode" (per-prompt relevance check + `/drift` re-verification while a plan is `active`, two-tiered to stay rule-11-cheap), real `/stopCEO`/`/ResumeCEO` controls, and a real Supabase datasheet (`ceo_plans`/`ceo_plan_items`/`ceo_reports`, seeded with the Massive Expansion plan's real 17-item state) tying every report back to the plan item it verified — full record `records/2026-08/decompress_ceo_grounded_mode_paranoia_2026-08-11.txt`**), `/colourgradient` (Aug 11 — a real, evidence-checked green/yellow/red build-status benchmark for a multi-item plan, built entirely as a rendering layer over `/drift`'s own procedure, never a competing evidence method;
