### Who you're working with
Alex (@AceSanyaBeats), UK music producer, Derby. Building RPGACE — a personal AI operating system for music production and content creation. Solo project.

### The governing rule
"Does this result in a beat made or video posted within 48 hours?" Yes → build now. No → wait. **Standing exception: RPGACE infrastructure itself is exempt** — building the system that builds beats counts.

### Architecture — never violate without explicit confirmation
- **main.js's freeze is LIFTED, indefinitely, as of Aug 6 (Alex's own explicit, verbatim call: "no more exceptions, im tired of the frozen core always being out of date and stale, the freeze is officially lifted indefinately").** Real, standing reason this was overdue, not just a mood: the freeze's own "deliberate logged exception" ceremony had made main.js accumulate real dead code invisibly — the Aug 6 dedup sweep found and removed a fully orphaned 4-agent "Content Pipeline" subsystem (~270 lines: `runPipeline`/`savePipelineToDB`/`addToEncyclopedia`/`copyPipelineOutput`/`resetPipeline`/`pipelineToNotion`/`pipelineToGitHub`/`pipelineToGmail`/`setAgentPipeStatus` + the `PIPELINE`/`TYPE_META`/`TYPE_PROMPTS` state) that had sat unreachable since its HTML host was removed from `index.html` at some earlier point, with no live DOM anchor left anywhere — the freeze discipline meant for safety had instead protected a genuinely dead subsystem from ever being cleaned up. `api/noter.js`, called only from that dead code, was deleted alongside it — a real, freed slot against the 12-Serverless-Function Vercel Hobby cap (now 11 of 12; see the landmine below). **Standing discipline going forward, unchanged**: still `node --check main.js` before every commit, still log real changes in patch_notes.html same session — the freeze's actual safety habits stay, only the "ask permission each time" ceremony is gone. `rpgace_core.js`'s own module-marker convention is untouched by this — new code still defaults there unless main.js is genuinely the right home for it.
- **All new code goes in rpgace_core.js** via `RPGACE.register()`, between `/* ===MODULE:x=== */`/`/* ===END:x=== */` markers. Direct Edit-tool edits following that marker convention are established precedent; always run `node --check rpgace_core.js` after every change.
- Working dir (local Windows machine): `C:\Users\acesa\Downloads\rpgace-vercel-v4`. Python automation scripts: `C:\Users\acesa\RPGACE`. Standing default since July 15: development happens in the cloud Claude Code session, which commits/pushes directly; the laptop is only needed for local-only Python scripts and `local_server.py` (port 7842).

### Known landmines — condensed, all still true
- **`oracle:response-scanned`'s `text` argument is `lastMsg.textContent` read AFTER `renderMarkdown()` has already rendered the reply into the chat DOM — a numbered/bulleted list is unrecoverable by the time any consumer sees it, and `addMsg`'s own "✦ Oracle" name label is glued directly onto the front of the string with no separator (Sep 13 2026, real live-production bug, found from Alex's own screenshot on the Cooking narrow-down feature — see the HABITS section's own full account).** `renderMarkdown()`'s `^\d+\. ` regex strips a numbered list's digit prefix outright, and DOM `textContent` never reinserts a newline for the block-display markup that separated the original list items — every EXISTING consumer of this hook (`RECIPE_JSON:`/`DIRECTOR_CHOSEN:`/`EDL_JSON:`/`ORACLE_ACTION:`) only ever hunts one inline marker followed by a single blob, so this never surfaced until a feature needed real multi-item list extraction for the first time. **Standing rule for any future consumer that needs Oracle to return a real LIST of distinct items (not one JSON blob or one trailer marker)**: ask for a single-line, delimiter-separated format (e.g. `" | "`-joined), never a numbered/bulleted list — a numbered list cannot survive this pipeline regardless of how the prompt is worded, since the stripping happens on the RENDERED DOM, not the raw reply. Also strip the known `"✦ Oracle"` label prefix before parsing from the very start of the string. **A stubbed-callback headless test (mocking `visualOracle._captureNextResponse` directly) will NOT catch either bug** — it never exercises the real `addMsg`→`renderMarkdown`→DOM→`textContent` pipeline; a real test must call the actual `addMsg()` and read back `lastMsg.textContent`, same lesson as the RECIPE_JSON/DIRECTOR_CHOSEN precedent this hook already serves.
- **Vercel Hobby caps a deployment at exactly 12 Serverless Functions — this project now sits at 11 of 12 (Aug 6, real dedup sweep freed 1 slot; `_context.js` excluded since Vercel's own convention doesn't count underscore-prefixed files).** Hit for real Aug 5 (Phase H): adding a 14th physical file (`api/bundle-deliverables.js`) made the production deploy come back `state: ERROR`, `errorCode: exceeded_serverless_functions_per_deployment` — caught by this project's own G6 deployment-reality check, not by Alex. **Aug 6: `api/noter.js` deleted** — real evidence found it had exactly one caller, the dead main.js `runPipeline()` chain removed the same session (see the main.js freeze-lifted bullet above); `api/scout.js`/`api/analyst.js` stay, both genuinely reused live by `rpgace_core.js`'s own ingestion path, confirmed via grep, not assumed. **Real, same-day G6 catch: deleting the file alone broke production** — `vercel.json` still explicitly listed `api/noter.js` in both its `rewrites` and `functions` blocks, and the deploy came back `state: ERROR` ("pattern `api/noter.js` defined in `functions` doesn't match any Serverless Functions"). Fixed same session (removed both stale entries, re-confirmed `READY`). **New standing rule this earned: deleting an `api/*.js` file needs a `grep -n "api/<name>" vercel.json` too, not just a codebase-wide grep for live callers** — a file can have zero JS/HTML callers and still be referenced by the deploy config alone. **Still never add a new `api/*.js` file without checking `ls api/*.js | wc -l` first.** If a new server-side job is genuinely needed, fold it into an existing endpoint with the closest matching real posture (pattern: `api/data-write.js`'s `action: 'bundle-deliverables'` branch, chosen because it already had the exact `requireAuth()` + service-role-key shape the new job needed) rather than adding a 13th real function — this is a zero-cost fix, not a reason to consider a Vercel Pro upgrade (Tier 3, Alex's call, not needed here). **Real, generalized instance of this same class, Aug 20 2026 — the Aug 20 `main.js`→`rpgace_core.js` merge left `graphify_river_group.py`'s hardcoded `Path('main.js')` (and a 2nd script's own separate hardcoded read) pointing at a deleted file, both failing silently OPEN rather than loud — the exact same "a codebase-wide caller grep isn't enough, a hardcoded path reference in TOOLING config/scripts is a real separate thing to check" lesson this bullet already names, just for Python generator scripts instead of `vercel.json`.** **A real 3rd instance, Aug 22 2026, found during a `/Bedtime` sweep, not caught at merge time**: `.claude/settings.json`'s own `check_commands` array still literally read `"node --check main.js"` two days after the file it names was deleted — a genuine harness-config blind spot the original merge pass and its own follow-up fixes never reached, since neither `graphify_river_group.py` nor `vercel.json` are the only places a hardcoded `main.js` path can hide. Fixed to `"node --check rpgace_core.js"`; `ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md`'s matching description corrected alongside it; logged as a real `achiever_archive` row (a stale claim, not broken code — the check command itself never ran and threw, it just silently checked a file that no longer existed). Full generalized workflow rule: `.claude/skills/CEO/SKILL.md` R19. **A real 4th instance, Aug 27 2026, found via a GitHub Actions failure-email Alex forwarded (not caught by any session in the 7 days since the merge)**: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`'s own "Check JS syntax" step still ran `node --check main.js && node --check rpgace_core.js` — the first half throwing `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` on every single push to `main` since Aug 20, failing the whole Action in ~9 seconds with exit code 1. **Real, load-bearing relief confirmed before fixing, not assumed**: this never affected the live app — Vercel's own native GitHub integration deploys every push to `main` independently of this Action (confirmed via `list_deployments`: every commit shows `state: READY` matching its real `githubCommitSha`, regardless of this workflow's own pass/fail history) — so this was a redundant, silently-broken 2nd deploy trigger sending failure emails for a week with zero real production impact, not a missed live-site outage. Fixed by dropping the dead `main.js` half of the check (reproduced the exact failure locally first, per rule 4, then re-verified the fixed command passes). **The generalized lesson keeps proving itself**: `vercel.json`, 2 Python generator scripts, `.claude/settings.json`, and now a GitHub Actions workflow — four independent hardcoded-`main.js`-reference sites, found one at a time across a week, each only surfaced by a different real trigger (a live deploy error, a `/Bedtime` sweep, an email). A single merge-time grep for `main.js` across the WHOLE repo (not just source/config files a session happens to be looking at) would have caught all four at once — worth remembering as the actual fix for this class of bug, not just patching each site as it's separately discovered.
- **A real Lighthouse/PageSpeed audit cannot be run from inside a Claude Code session against rpgace.vercel.app (confirmed July 28, two independent methods, not transient).** PageSpeed Insights API returns `quota_limit_value: 0` for this environment's anonymous consumer — a hard restriction, not a rate limit that clears with a retry. A locally-run Lighthouse (via the pre-installed Chromium) hits a real Chrome interstitial, traced to this session's own outbound proxy blocking raw network access to the live site (`curl` alone returns `CONNECT tunnel failed, 403`, independent of Lighthouse). Don't re-attempt either path in a future session — this is genuinely Alex's own 30-second task (Chrome DevTools' built-in Lighthouse panel, or pagespeed.web.dev, from his own browser), not something Claude Code can complete standing alone despite how cheap it looks on a backlog list.
- **Never add a static `<script>` tag to `index.html`.** Exactly ONE client script, `rpgace_core.js` (as of Aug 20 2026 — see the real, verified merge note below; `main.js` was mechanically absorbed into it, so this landmine's original "a third breaks the password gate via race conditions" risk is now even further from ever being hit than before). Runtime-injected scripts via `document.createElement('script')` (e.g. Bookworm's PDF.js loader `_ensurePdfJs()`) are the approved pattern for extra libraries.
- **Real, verified merge, Aug 20 2026 — `main.js` was mechanically absorbed into `rpgace_core.js`, Alex's own direct ask ("get rid of main.js... absorb it into core.js"), zero logic rewrite.** Real evidence gathered before touching anything (rule 1): `rpgace_core.js`'s own IIFE wrapper (`(function(global){'use strict';...})(window)`) meant a naive paste-inside-the-closure would have silently broken every one of `main.js`'s 211 top-level global functions — including all 47 distinct names `index.html`'s inline `onclick="fnName(...)"` handlers depend on. Fixed by placing `main.js`'s content verbatim OUTSIDE the IIFE (a real `/* ===LEGACY:mainjs=== */`/`/* ===END:LEGACY:mainjs=== */` marked section near the top of the file, byte-diff-verified against the old `main.js`), preserving every global exactly as before. 17 real "collisions" between the two files' own names turned out to be the ALREADY-DOCUMENTED, deliberate `window.X` override pattern (`main.js` defines an original/stub, a `rpgace_core.js` module's `init()` reassigns it) — confirmed safe because the override code runs inside a deferred `onReady()` callback, not at parse time, so file-boundary timing never mattered. `main.js`/`main.min.js` deleted (git history preserves them); `scripts/minify_client.js` now targets one file. **Real, load-bearing follow-up fix, same pass**: the merge shifted every line in the original `rpgace_core.js` content down by exactly 4451 lines — `scripts/graphify_river_group.py`'s `MAIN_JS = Path('main.js')` hardcoded read (the exact mechanism behind the Aug 14 UI/backend detection fix, `compute_mainjs_window_bridge()`) would have silently gone blind (fails open to `{}`, no crash) without a matching fix; replaced with `_legacy_mainjs_text()`, which extracts the same content from the new sentinel markers inside `rpgace_core.js` itself — functionally re-verified (`authGate`'s own real bridge, the Aug 14 fix's own worked example, still detects correctly). A second real perf regression (reading+regex-scanning the now much bigger merged file on every call, unmemoized, timed out `galaxy_map_module.py`/`galaxy_map_zoom.py`) was fixed with a module-level cache on `_legacy_mainjs_text()`. `perspective_generate_onclick_features.py`'s own separate hardcoded `main.js` read was fixed the same way (imports the shared helper, rule 8). 6 hand-cited line-number anchors across `galaxy_map_decisions.py`/`galaxy_map_level5.py` (the anchor-verification system's own standing "fails loud, never silently stale" design working exactly as intended) were re-computed against live current line numbers — some by the flat +4451 shift, several by real independent drift from other same-session edits, individually re-verified. Full 21-script Galaxy Map pipeline regenerated: idempotent (2 fresh runs, identical md5 across all 22 files), div/a/svg-balanced, headless-Chromium-verified zero console errors on every touched page.
- **Never use Python string-replace on `index.html`** — `exportEncyclopedia` contains a fake `</body></html>` inside a JS string literal. Targeted edits only.
- **All fixed overlays** (modals, popups, panels) are created via `document.createElement` and appended to `document.body` — never inside `.page` divs, which intercept clicks via z-index even when `display:none` in some browsers.
- **Every `id="page-X"` div** must appear inside `#app`, before the script tags.
- **`RPGACE.sb.insert()` does NOT return the inserted row** (`Prefer: return=minimal`, response never parsed). If you need the new row's `id`, use raw `fetch` with `Prefer: return=representation` and check `response.ok` — the pattern in `phylumPath._insertNewSteps()` and `bookworm._createBookFromExtraction()`. This exact mistake caused Bookworm's "Chapter 0 of 0" silent-failure bug (July 17).
- **`RPGACE.hooks.fire()` never revisits listeners added mid-fire.** Any module calling `hooks.on('rpgace:ready', ...)` from inside its own `init()` registers too late and silently never fires. When in doubt, call the function directly — `'rpgace:ready'` has always already fired by the time `init()` runs. **The "~25 unaudited occurrences" backlog this bullet used to carry is CLOSED** (re-audited July 29, re-confirmed by grep July 31: exactly **one** occurrence remains in `rpgace_core.js`, and it is `RPGACE.register()`'s own canonical module-init machinery, not a bug site). The behaviour is still true and still worth knowing; there is no backlog attached to it.
- **A dashDeck stash-guard cannot tell "popup open" from "popup closing"** — every `onClose` must pass `force=true` to `_stashWidget(id, force)`. This bug was hit twice in one day. Related z-index contract: dashDeck popups sit at **99999**, above the 9998-10110 side-panel/drawer/toast tier, so any button inside a dashDeck popup that opens one of those lower surfaces must call `closeWidgetPopup(id)` first or it opens behind the popup.
- **There is no Supabase backup and no point-in-time recovery, at all** (Alex confirmed directly — a settled fact, not an open question). **Real, closed decision, Sep 15 2026 (`/paranoia` pass): the org (`acesanyabeats-RPGACE`) is confirmed on Supabase's Free plan; Alex explicitly chose to stay Free and accept the risk rather than upgrade for backups/PITR — asked directly, not re-flagged again.** Do not re-raise this as an open question in a future session; the real safety net is the existing discipline (evidence-gather before any destructive SQL, verify counts before/after) — worth remembering before any destructive data operation regardless, because there is still nothing to restore from if that discipline ever lapses.
- **Fable 5 can run out of usage credits mid-task** and will fail with a plain task-notification, not a silent hang. Confirmed twice on real dispatches. When it happens, report the failure and ask — do NOT substitute a different model into the Fable role without Alex's explicit say-so (both times his answer was to substitute Sonnet, but that was his call to make each time).
- **Writes done via raw `fetch`** (like `saveOracleToEncyclopedia`) bypass `RPGACE.cache`'s cache-busting wrappers; call `RPGACE.cache.clear('tablename')` manually after them or reads can be stale up to 60s.
- **Word-boundary regex treats hyphens as boundaries** — bare keywords like `roll` match inside `b-roll`. Keyword lists use compound phrases, never bare adjectives; this convention is what prevents cross-phylum collisions (verified across 4 phylum-pair checks).
- **A fix on a feature branch is not a fix Alex can test.** `rpgace.vercel.app` deploys from `main`. A commit sitting only on a working branch (e.g. `claude/rpgace-master-steps-uobp98`) will never reach the live app no matter how correct it is. Confirmed real July 17: the Bookworm TOC-extraction retry fix sat unmerged for a full round while Alex re-tested production and reproduced the identical bug, because the fix had never actually gone live. **Standing rule: merge to `main` and push `main` BEFORE telling Alex something is ready to test — every time, no exceptions.** If `main` and the working branch have diverged, resolve that first; never push a conflict live. **Hit again, much worse, Aug 11**: an entire session's real work (6 commits — the massive-expansion spec, `/drift`, `/CEO`, prompt caching, `session_memory`, the whole A17 oversight restructuring) was reported as "shipped"/"pushed"/"live" throughout, while every commit sat on the feature branch only — `main` was never touched. Alex caught it by checking the live site directly and finding nothing had changed. **The word "pushed" is not the same claim as "merged to main"** — a session can push a feature branch correctly and still leave Alex with nothing to test; saying "shipped" or "live" without having just checked `git log origin/main` for the actual commit is an unverified claim, same as any other rule-1 violation. Verify with the Vercel API (`list_deployments`, confirm the real commit SHA is the `READY` production deployment) before using words like "live" — don't infer it from a successful `git push`.
- **`index.html`'s `?v=` cache-bust query strings on `main.js`/`rpgace_core.js` must be bumped in the SAME commit that changes either file.** Found stale Aug 11 (both strings still read `20260806` after `rpgace_core.js` had changed multiple times same session) — a real second layer of the "nothing changed" symptom on top of the unmerged-branch bug above: even a correctly deployed `main` can keep serving an old cached copy of the script to a browser that already loaded the page once. Check this specifically whenever either file changes, not just when a bug report implies caching.
- **As of Aug 11 2026, `index.html` serves `main.min.js`/`rpgace_core.min.js`, not the raw `main.js`/`rpgace_core.js` — this rule now has a real second half.** `main.js`/`rpgace_core.js` remain the true, edited, fully-commented SOURCE (always edit those, never the `.min.js` files directly) — but they are no longer what ships. Run `node scripts/minify_client.js` (real, verified-safe: `compress:true, mangle:false` — mangle is permanently disabled because `index.html` has 93 inline `onclick="fnName(...)"` attributes a minifier's per-file static analysis cannot see, and renaming any of those 47 distinct referenced globals would silently break that button) in the SAME commit as any change to either source file, and bump BOTH `.min.js` cache-bust `?v=` strings too. A stale `.min.js` next to a changed source file is the exact same "nothing changed" bug as an un-bumped `?v=` — now with a second file that can independently go stale. `scripts/minify_client.js` itself fails loud (`node --check` on its own output) if a bad minify ever produces invalid JS, but it cannot catch "forgot to re-run it at all" — that stays a discipline check, same as everywhere else this class of landmine appears in this file.
- **`openmontage_jobs` (July 31) must NEVER be added to a future Approach B RLS-restriction batch without giving the separate OpenMontage Claude Code session another way to write to it.** Same exact failure mode as the `oracle_fallback_queue` warning immediately below: that external session writes back with the plain anon key (no service-role key, no `RPGACE_API_SECRET` — those are private to this codebase and never handed to a separate session). Flipping this table's RLS the way `bookworm_*`/`taxonomy_*` were flipped would silently 403 every write-back, leaving jobs stuck at `status='queued'` forever with no visible error.
- **`error_log`/`smoke_test_items`/`ceo_plan_items` must NEVER be restricted to `anon_read_only` — all three have a real, live, confirmed anon-key write from the deployed browser app, same landmine class as `oracle_fallback_queue`/`openmontage_jobs` (Sep 15 2026, `/paranoia` pass, confirmed via direct grep before a planned RLS tightening pass — 5 sibling meta tables WERE safely restricted the same session, see Skills/tooling/security above).** `error_log`: `RPGACE.sb.update`/a raw anon-key `fetch(...POST)` inside `errorLog._capture` — every real error-toast/dedup write goes through this. `smoke_test_items`: `RPGACE.sb.update` in both the G109 auto-broken-flip cascade and Alex's own real hand-confirm click. `ceo_plan_items`: `RPGACE.sb.update` in the G109 auto-purple-flip-on-regression cascade. Restricting any of these three to `anon_read_only` would silently 403 the real write with no visible error — check this specifically before ever touching these 3 tables' RLS.
- **`oracle_fallback_queue` (July 24) must NEVER be added to a future Approach B RLS-restriction batch without also giving the "RPGACE Fallback Drain" Routine a way to still write to it.** The Routine writes back via curl with the plain anon/publishable key (no MCP connectors, no access to `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` or `RPGACE_API_SECRET`) — exactly like the Morning Brief Routine already does for `journal`. If this table's RLS is ever flipped to anon-read-only the way `bookworm_*`/`taxonomy_*` were in Approach B phase 1, the Routine's writes would start silently 403'ing — no crash, no visible error to Alex, just rows that stay `status='pending'`/`'answered'` forever with no `resumed_at`. Check this specifically before ever touching this table's RLS again.
- **The whole Claude-fallback lane has never been exercised against a real credit-exhaustion event.** `phylumPath._isCreditExhaustionError`'s string match (`"credit balance"` / `"insufficient credit"`) is a best guess at Anthropic's real error wording, verified only by reading `api/_context.js`'s error-propagation code, never against a live failure — forcing one on purpose would mean actually draining Alex's own API credits. It fails open by design (a wrong guess just means the feature silently never triggers, same as before it existed — never a crash or a wrong queue), but this means the feature's real behavior is UNCONFIRMED. The first genuine signal will be whenever credits actually run low; check `oracle_fallback_queue` and the Fallback Drain Routine's own run history at that point, don't assume it worked.
- **A parse failure on a fallback answer leaves a row silently stuck.** If the Fallback Drain Routine's own Claude session doesn't format an answer exactly as expected (e.g. wraps JSON in explanation text despite being told not to), `resumeFallbackPlacement`'s parsing can fail — the row stays `status='answered'` with `resumed_at` still null, gets retried by `_checkFallbackAnswers`'s sweep every 5 minutes, and fails the same way every time with only a `console.warn` (no toast, nothing Alex would ever see unless he opens devtools). Check `oracle_fallback_queue` directly for old `answered_at` rows with `resumed_at IS NULL` if the fallback lane is ever suspected of not working.
- **Bookworm's auto-resume only covers a cascade's FIRST placement attempt per phylum**, not a reword-retry or upgrade-check sub-call inside `_placeInsightCascade` — a credit-exhaustion failure during one of those sub-stages falls through to the pre-existing swallow-to-null behavior, unchanged, not queued/resumed. Real, accepted scope limit, not a bug — extending coverage to every sub-stage would be real additional work if it ever proves necessary.
- **Oct 14 2026: the recurring ~669ms "App froze" alert is the boot script load (phone-speed profile), now recorded without alerting; real causes of any later freeze land in `perf_freezes` — read it before any freeze work.** **The swipe-gesture freeze — real, superseding update, Sep 22 2026 (`/Routine` evidence pass): the freeze HAS recurred, extensively, and one of this bullet's own prior claims is corrected.** The Sep 15 bullet below (kept for its own historical narrative) said a live `error_log` query for "freeze"/"longtask"/"swipe" had come up empty — re-checked directly Sep 22 and that is no longer true: `error_log` row `81bcf07f-bbda-46ea-b684-6b290a2c726c` (signature `ui_toast|toast()|⚠️ App froze for <n>ms (logged)`) shows `first_seen_at` 2026-08-27 (the day after G109 shipped), **re-queried Sep 23 2026 (`/Routine` pass): `last_seen_at` now 2026-09-23, 76 real occurrences** (up from 69/Sep 20 — still firing, same bug class, not yet correlated to a specific page/action) — it started firing immediately and has kept firing for nearly a month. **Real correction to this bullet's own prior claim**: "it should already hold the real duration + a stack" is FALSE for the stack half, confirmed by direct read of `perfWatch.init`'s `PerformanceObserver('longtask')` listener (`rpgace_core.js`) — only the rounded `ms` duration is ever passed into the toast/error_log capture; the browser's native Long Task API exposes no call stack, and `console.warn`'s own richer `entry` object is devtools-only, never persisted. A future session should not expect a stack to already be there. **Severity read honestly, not alarmed**: the latest captured occurrence is 669ms — nowhere near the original ~12,265ms report; `error_log`'s own `message` field only ever shows the LATEST occurrence's exact ms, so whether any of the other 68 were far worse is genuinely unknown from this data alone. This may be routine main-thread work (a large render, the minified-file parse on boot) tripping the `>=500ms` threshold rather than a reproduction of the original dramatic freeze — real, recurring, and worth knowing, but not yet re-confirmed as the SAME bug. **A real, separate data-integrity bug found on this same row, also fixed Sep 22**: its `attempts` array held a full investigation narrative that was actually about a completely different, already-closed bug (the Aug 27 quest-creation "Unexpected token '<'" / SSO issue, closed Sep 4) — dated the same day as this row's own `first_seen_at`, almost certainly from a prior session running a manual UPDATE against this row id instead of inserting a fresh one for the new real symptom. Cleared and replaced with an honest note so a future session doesn't read the wrong investigation history against the wrong bug. **Standing call, still unchanged**: don't attempt a 5th blind code pass on `leftNav._initSwipeGesture` — it's still the confirmed-clean `touchstart`/`touchend` pair from July 28. If a genuinely severe (multi-second) freeze recurs, check `error_log` for the real captured duration first; a stack was never built and isn't available.
- **The swipe-gesture freeze — deprioritized July 28 with a real reason, not silently dropped. Real, superseding update, Sep 15 2026 (`/paranoia` fresh-look pass): the "no stored evidence" half of the blocker is now CLOSED, though the bug itself still isn't found.** Re-checked rather than assumed: `leftNav._initSwipeGesture` (unchanged by the Sep 2026 G53 ui/logic split — confirmed by its own code comment, a pure structural move) still has exactly `touchstart`/`touchend`, both `{passive:true}`, no synchronous heavy work in either — the July 28 finding holds, still no 5th blind code pass attempted. The genuinely new fact: `perfWatch`'s freeze toast (`RPGACE.utils.toast('⚠️ App froze...', '#CC4A4A', 4000, 'perfWatch')`) is error-colored and passes its own module name — since Aug 26 2026 (G109), EVERY `#CC4A4A` toast automatically persists into `error_log` via `errorLog._capture`, no manual step required. Confirmed via direct code read (`toast()`'s `if (color === '#CC4A4A')` branch, `rpgace_core.js`), not assumed from the commit message alone. A live `error_log` query for "freeze"/"longtask"/"swipe" found zero rows — meaning either it genuinely hasn't recurred since Aug 26, or Alex hasn't hit it on a build with this wired; either way, the standing ask below is now simpler than it used to be. **Standing call, revised**: don't attempt a 5th blind code pass. Next time the freeze recurs, check `error_log` for a `perfWatch`-sourced row FIRST — it should already hold the real duration + a stack, no screen recording or manual `console.trace()` needed anymore. Only fall back to asking Alex for a live screen recording if `error_log` comes up empty despite a real repro.

### Non-negotiable process rules
1. **Pull real source before editing.** Never guess at function internals.
2. **Get real evidence before positioning/layout code** — screenshot or real DOM output.
3. **Grep before adding new UI behavior** — this project has a repeated history of duplicate implementations.
4. **One failed fix = stop, get real evidence, don't patch blind a second time.** July 17's hardest-won version of this: when a bug seems to resist a fix that should have worked, **query Supabase directly** (check `source_url`, row counts, null columns) to confirm which code path actually produced the data — the recurring "TOC saved as chapter 1" frustration was the user unknowingly using a different entry point than the one being fixed. **This explicitly covers any review-queue-shaped backlog too** (`taxonomy_proposals`, `taxonomy_links`, `intel_reports`/Content Intelligence's proposal pipeline, `oracle_dev_suggestions`) — a prior audit's characterization of a backlog ("largely garbage," "107 items") is itself a claim to re-verify against live rows, not a fact to act on directly (real July 24 example: a "60 pending, largely bad" backlog turned out to be two genuinely different batches by real engine/score/timestamp evidence — treating it as uniform would have been wrong either direction).
5. **Multi-subsystem asks (3+ new pieces) get spec'd and confirmed first.** Record real answers verbatim in a committed .txt backlog file (pattern: `bookworm_spec_backlog.txt`) — never substitute recommended defaults for the user's actual answers. **Same convention applies to resolving a real backlog-clearing decision, not just a build decision** — when real evidence-gathering + interrogation resolves what to do with a live backlog (pattern: `item7_process_debate_2026-07-24.txt`), it gets its own dedicated verbatim file too, not just prose folded into the oversight docs.
6. **Every confirmed idea or fix updates the Oversight docs in the same session.** Not optional, not batched. **The actual mechanism, named Aug 6 after this exact rule was followed for six docs and still missed `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE`**: the `.claude/skills/update-logging-system/SKILL.md` dependency map (change type → required artifact, including `SELF_KNOWLEDGE` and any skill `.md` file whose behavior evolved — both previously unnamed by this rule's own prose) is the checklist that makes this obligation checkable instead of just stated. Run through `Bedtime`'s Step 1, or directly for any Tier 2+ mid-session update.
7. **Copyright line, held twice on July 17 and standing:** never fetch/wire in book content from torrent-style listings, "complete with files" bundles, or generic multi-book file directories (shadow libraries), regardless of a stated purchase elsewhere. Legitimate paths: the user's own purchased/downloaded file (PDF upload exists for exactly this), genuine public-domain full-text links, or manual entry from an owned copy.
8. **Eliminate deduplication gaps — always, no exceptions (Alex-confirmed July 23, term corrected same day — his own verbatim wording said "reduplication," a real word but the wrong one; he meant "deduplication," the term this project's own code already uses, e.g. `intelDedup`):** "reduplication is always a priority, whether functions or transportation or changing data, same processes must go through one pipeline or function if steps are identical" [verbatim, kept as-said]. Two real, already-distinct patterns this covers, both load-bearing: **(a) CODE duplication** — if two files/modules do the same real work (fetch the same kind of data, run the same check, hold the same lookup table), they route through ONE shared function/pipeline, never two hand-rolled copies, even if that means importing a helper instead of pasting a few lines; and **(b) DATA deduplication** — the same discipline already proven working in this codebase: `intelDedup` (normalized-URL + title-fallback dedup for Content Intelligence rows, shipped July 19) and the taxonomy placement engine's per-chapter leaf-name dedup (`_continueAnalyzingInBackground`'s "batch-dedup awareness," also July 19 — stops a chapter from minting near-duplicate taxonomy leaves for the same real concept). Both existed before this rule was named; naming the rule doesn't invent new work, it recognizes and extends a pattern Alex already asked for twice independently. Real cost of skipping (a), found July 23: `api/composio.js` had its own hand-rolled copy of `_context.js`'s CORS headers AND its own divergent `ACCOUNTS` map — the two had drifted apart (composio.js's gmail/instagram ids were the newer, correct ones), meaning `executor.js`/`orchestrate.js` had been silently calling the WRONG connected-account ids since a June 28 Composio update, with no error, no warning, just quietly wrong data for two real integrations. Skipping either (a) or (b) isn't just untidy — it's a live correctness bug waiting to happen the moment two copies (of code OR data) diverge, and there's no way to know which one is "correct" from the code alone. When auditing or building anything, actively grep for a second copy — of a function, a lookup table, or a data row — before assuming one doesn't exist. (`taxonomy_nodes` vs. `taxonomy_tree` is NOT an instance of this — that split is a deliberate, already-reasoned architecture decision per `interconnection_map.md`'s own explicit note, not an accidental duplication; don't conflate the two.)
9. **Repeated identical interrupt/continue cycles: one reload is normal, a second one is the stop condition (Alex-confirmed July 28, corrected same session by voice — the first version of this rule said "8+ times," too loose; Alex tied the real threshold to `/Engineer`'s own existing per-defect cap: "if the same defect survives a second fix attempt, the loop stops immediately").** This is a client/harness artifact (9 consecutive "[Request interrupted by user for tool use] Continue from where you left off." system messages fired in one real occurrence, with no new input from Alex), not him retyping the same thing on purpose. A single reload/retry of the same auto-continue message is fine — noise, not a problem. But the moment the exact same cycle starts a SECOND time with no genuine new content: stop immediately and **finish** — don't just checkpoint-and-wait, bring the current unit of work to a real, reportable conclusion (write the real state to a dated checkpoint `.txt` file per rule 5's verbatim-record convention if there's a genuine open question, then report status) rather than attempting a third pass. Same discipline as `/Engineer`'s own re-looping guardrail, applied to this specific harness-artifact case, not a separate invented threshold.
10. **When a step needs Alex's own hands, hand him a verification, not just an instruction (Alex-confirmed Aug 4, during the OpenMontage/Kaggle build).** His own framing, verbatim intent: he often believes a step is done when it isn't, and the assistant already knows what "actually done" would look like — that knowledge has to travel WITH the instruction, not stay implicit. Two real, costly examples from the same session this rule was named in: (a) a PowerShell command to save a Kaggle token "succeeded" with zero errors while actually writing the literal placeholder text `YOUR_REAL_TOKEN` instead of his real value — caught only because the assistant had already specified a follow-up `Get-Content` check, not because anything looked wrong on its own; (b) Kaggle's phone-verification requirement was mentioned once as a side-note with no stated "here's how you'll know it's done," and its silent absence blocked a real 21.5GB dataset finalization for nearly an hour — genuine debugging time spent suspecting a broken upload before the real cause (an unverified account, invisible to the CLI) was found on the actual website UI. **Standing rule: any instruction handed to Alex to execute himself — a terminal command, an account setting, a UI click, anything outside what a session can run directly — must ship with a concrete, checkable verification in the same message.** Not "let me know how it goes" — a specific command to run and what it should print, or a specific page/section to check and what it should show, decided in advance by the assistant so "I think it's done" becomes real evidence instead of an assumption. Same discipline rule 4 already applies to the assistant's own claims, extended to the steps only Alex can physically perform.
12. **JS single-quoted strings escape an apostrophe with `\'`, never `''` (Alex's own enhanced-Bedtime ask, Aug 11, real mistake caught mid-session).** Specific scenario: editing `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` — a JS single-quoted string containing real apostrophes ("doesn't fit," "'s wrong") — a `''` (SQL-style escaping, muscle memory from writing SQL string literals the same session) was used instead of the correct JS `\'`, and `node --check` failed with `SyntaxError: Unexpected identifier`. Caught immediately by the standing "run `node --check` after every edit" habit, not by inspection. **Broader RPGACE Total-system rule**: any session doing BOTH SQL work (Supabase migrations/UPDATEs) and JS string-literal edits in the same pass is at real risk of cross-contaminating escaping conventions between the two languages — when editing a JS string literal that will contain an apostrophe, deliberately think "backslash, not doubled-quote" before typing it, and always run `node --check` immediately after, not batched at the end of several edits.
13. **A renumber/relabel task needs TWO separate greps — display text AND the raw data-write literal — never one (Alex's own enhanced-Bedtime ask, Aug 11, real near-miss caught this session).** Specific scenario: renumbering RPGACE's taxonomy phyla, a grep for the display-text pattern `Phylum 1[1-4]\b` found and fixed every user-facing/prompt string, but `beatLog._addNewArtistsToTaxonomy` had a hardcoded `phylum_number: 12` object-literal value with no adjacent "Phylum 12" text anywhere near it (only a comment naming the phylum by NAME, not number) — a second, separate grep for the raw literal pattern (`phylum_number:\s*\d+`) was what actually caught it, after the first grep had already been declared complete. Had this shipped unfixed, new Last.fm-discovered artists would have been silently written into the WRONG phylum with zero error, indefinitely. **Broader RPGACE Total-system rule**: any task that renumbers, renames, or re-IDs something with both a display representation and a raw code/data representation (phylum numbers, table IDs, model identifiers, status enum values) must grep for BOTH the human-readable pattern AND the raw literal-assignment pattern as two genuinely separate searches before declaring the migration complete — a text-only grep systematically misses literals sitting in code with no nearby label.
14. **Verify `git branch --show-current` returns `main` before every commit, not just the first one of a session (Alex's own enhanced-Bedtime ask, Aug 11, real harness quirk hit mid-session).** Specific scenario: a `git commit` mid-session unexpectedly landed on `claude/rpgace-master-steps-uobp98` instead of `main`, with no explicit `git checkout` issued by the assistant — a harness auto-checkout, diagnosed via `git branch --show-current`/`git log --oneline` and reconciled with `git checkout main && git merge --ff-only <branch> && git push origin main` (a fast-forward, no work lost, but only because it was caught the same turn). **Broader RPGACE Total-system rule, extending the existing "merge to main before saying it's ready to test" landmine**: that existing landmine assumed the assistant always knows which branch it's on; this session proved that assumption can be silently wrong even without the assistant issuing a branch-switching command. Check the current branch immediately before EVERY commit in a session, not just at session start — a harness-level branch switch can happen invisibly between commits, and the existing standing rule (never tell Alex something is ready to test without confirming `main` has the real commit) only holds if this check runs every time.
15. **The Galaxy Map is the baseline evidence source for "where does X live in RPGACE Total systems" questions — grep supplements it for details it doesn't compute, it doesn't replace it (Alex's own direct ask, Aug 15: "use galaxy map for this as a baseline rule instead of grepping, or use both in tandem... help with quality of findings, output of changes and functions themselves, and token usage").** Real worked example, same session: investigating A5 (Research Lab dismantling) by grepping `rpgace_core.js` cold would have meant re-deriving module/river ownership from scratch. Querying `scripts/graphify_river_group.py`'s own already-computed `RIVER_MODULES`/`DASHBOARD_CARDS` tables instead answered the real question in 2 cheap Python calls: `conidPot`/`beatLog`/`contentProductionLive`/`videoSummary` are ALREADY River XI (Content Pipeline) modules, `refCorpus` is ALREADY River VII (grouped with `encTaxonomyLink` — i.e. already taxonomy-tree-adjacent, matching A5's own "corpus should feed the taxonomy tree" ask), and the single "🧠 Research Lab" dashboard card is the ONLY thing still fanning all 7 tabs' worth of already-correctly-owned content through one River-V-branded door (confirmed via `researchTabs._resolveSections()` — it doesn't own the Idea Bank/Corpus/Beat Log/Workshop/Bibliography content at all, it just shows/hides fixed DOM ids that OTHER modules already inject into). This reframed a feared "backend rewrite" into a much smaller, lower-risk "add dashboard cards / re-route UI" problem — a materially better finding, reached for a fraction of the token cost a cold grep sweep would have taken. **Standing rule**: before grepping `rpgace_core.js` cold for "what module owns X" / "what river is X in" / "what already exists near X," check the Galaxy Map's own data first (`graphify-out/galaxy_map*.html` for a rendered read, or import `scripts/graphify_river_group.py` directly for a scriptable query against `RIVER_MODULES`/`DASHBOARD_CARDS`/`CARDS_BY_RIVER`/`EXTERNAL_RIVER_LINKS` etc.) — fall back to a targeted grep only for the specific implementation detail the map doesn't capture (e.g. a tab's own internal DOM-id wiring), never as the first move for a relational/ownership question.

16. **A doc can be made false by a change made somewhere else entirely — so name the SUBJECT that changed and check the 2-4 docs that plausibly claim something about it (Aug 23 2026, DD6/DD9 of the ratified "Oversight Doc Drift Discipline & Plan-First Restructure" plan).** Real trigger: Alex's own hand-test found `future_integrations.html` asserting "Research Lab page confirmed fully intact, untouched" long after that page was retired, while CLAUDE.md described the same feature accurately and the real `ceo_plan_items` row said a third thing again — three sources of truth silently diverged for over a week. Two more instances surfaced in the same pass: five `smoke_test_items` rows pointing at Galaxy Map pages deleted in the Aug 21 fold passes (those passes correctly repointed every cross-reference INSIDE the map pages, but the rows live in Supabase, outside the file-grep blast radius), and one row invalidated purely by a file it CITED being deleted, with the feature it tested never changing at all. **Two halves, deliberately separate.** **(a) Structural, and always preferred: where a doc can be RENDERED from real data instead of asserted by hand, do that.** `future_integrations.html`'s stale card was only possible because it was hand-typed HTML sitting next to a live-fetched section; making every card live-fetched removed the entire class of bug rather than adding a rule to remember. A checklist is the fallback for what genuinely cannot be rendered, never the first tool reached for. **(b) Procedural, for what must be asserted by hand: the Cross-Doc Drift Check** (`.claude/skills/update-logging-system/SKILL.md` artifact 20, its own named section) — after a real change, name the specific checkable SUBJECT that is now different (a count, a module list, a feature's built/not-built state, a file's existence, a plan item's status), look only at the artifacts that plausibly carry a claim about that same subject, grade any real inconsistency with `/drift`'s own VERDICT/BASIS/MINOR-MATERIAL-CAPTURED vocabulary, and route it to the mechanism that already owns the outcome (a stale CLAIM → achiever.html brown; broken CODE → error_log.html purple; neither → fix in place). **Explicitly NOT a full pairwise sweep of all 12 oversight docs on every push** — that costs more than the drift it catches, and rule 11 still applies. Wired into `/CEO` (R24), `/Summary`, `/Bedtime`, and — newly obligated, where they carried nothing before — `/scope`, GODMODE (`omnitrix`), and `/commit-archaeologist`; all six point at the one definition rather than carrying a copy (rule 8). **Real proof it works, from its own first run**: applied to the session that built it, it immediately caught a genuine stale claim in this very file (the `future_integrations.html` paragraph still said "grouped by color" hours after DD2 made it plan-first). **The generalizable half worth remembering beyond the checklist**: a writer's attention follows the file being edited, not the files that merely describe it — and a real reference can live in a Supabase row or a tooling config, not just a tracked file, which is the same lesson `vercel.json`, `graphify_river_group.py`, and `.claude/settings.json` each taught once already.

17. **A `/CEO` Loop 2 execution that bypasses `/Engineer`'s own Stage 5 Truth Check is not a substitute for it, even when the orchestrating session's own review is thorough (Aug 23 2026, same session as rule 16, found while shipping DD8).** Real, confirmed instance: DD8 was dispatched as a custom Omnitrix build (Opus builds, Sonnet reviews) rather than routed through `/Engineer`'s full 5-stage loop, despite CEO SKILL.md's own Loop 2 Step 1 already saying every item runs through `/Engineer`'s "real build-verify-report cycle... a real Truth Check before anything is called done." The orchestrating session's own manual diff review WAS thorough — it independently re-verified module counts, row counts, and div-balance against live evidence — but it still missed a real internal self-contradiction the build's own agent introduced into `patch_notes.html`: one sentence said "Seven `achiever_archive` rows logged," a later sentence in the SAME card said "21 of 21 rows backfilled... with one honest blank" (which itself is self-contradicting — 21/21 implies zero blanks). The true number, confirmed by direct Supabase query, was 9. Free-form structural review checks whether a diff does what it claims; it does not systematically re-derive every hard number a build's own new prose asserts and cross-check it against every OTHER place in the SAME commit that states the same fact — which is precisely `/Engineer` Stage 5's named discipline ("re-derive the real state from scratch," never trusting the report's own memory), and precisely what a custom dispatch skips by not invoking it. **Workflow, now standing**: a `/CEO` Loop 2 item is not "done" until it has actually gone through `/Engineer`'s own Stage 5 (or 5b) — a custom Agent-tool dispatch plus an ad hoc orchestrator review, however careful, does not satisfy Loop 2 Step 1, and should be named as a real deviation if it happens rather than silently treated as equivalent. `/Engineer`'s Stage 5 itself gained an explicit **Self-Consistency Sweep** for exactly this failure class (`.claude/skills/Engineer/SKILL.md`) — grep every hard number a build's own new doc/commit-message content asserts and confirm it agrees with itself before confirming it agrees with reality.

18. **Brief Alex before starting any plan step (Alex, Oct 11 2026: "before commiting a plan step, give me a breif on whats planned so im always included").** Before building any item of a ratified `/CEO` plan (or any step of a multi-step build), post a short plain-language brief first: what the step is and where it came from, what will be built and what it will change for him, any open choices with a recommendation, and what is deliberately left out. Then wait for his go-ahead. A plan item being ratified weeks ago is not the same as him knowing what it is today (real trigger: S1 "Screenshot inbox" was approved Oct 10 as "Yes, later", and on Oct 11 he didn't recognise it when a session went straight to build questions).

Restructured per Alex's own explicit redefinition (A17 of `alex_critique_and_massive_expansion_spec_2026-08-10.txt`) — the original 7 file-based docs (now 12, see the header's own correction above), genuinely split by what each one actually does, plus 4 layers extending the system beyond files. Deployed at rpgace.vercel.app, also in this project root:

### Tier (a) — "Shown/explaining docs," feed off Tier (b), plain language for Alex
- **patch_notes.html** — unchanged role: day-by-day build history, F-series roadmap (F0 onward — the old R-items/36-step list are historical), every bug found/fixed. The most recent "session ended here" / finish-line card is the honest current-state summary. **Real scope decision, Aug 12**: the full error TIMELINE (every fix attempt, why each failed) and the resolved LESSON now live on `error_log.html` instead of here — Alex's own real correction mid-spec, to keep this doc's own job narrower ("de-clunk and narrow the specialisation of patch notes"). This doc still gets a short, real card when an error is actually resolved (what shipped, one paragraph) — the full forensic detail is `error_log.html`'s job, not duplicated here (rule 8).
- **manual.html is RETIRED, Sep 23 2026 (Minotaur/Manual Unification, `ceo_plans` id `96c21d7f-1dfd-49d2-8479-1dacdaed991e`, real Alex-ratified plan — "ratify the plan, keep manual content in Tier b docs").** Real `/interrogation`+`/drift` pass first (record: `records/2026-09/minotaur_manual_unification_ceo_interrogation_2026-09-23.txt`). Its dev-facing content is relocated, not deleted (git history preserves the original file): the button catalog + Oracle background capabilities + the architecture/build-workflow section (confirmed `rpgace_build.py` is still real and live before the move) → `interconnection_map.md`; the detailed per-table Supabase reference → `system_flow_map.md`'s §0 (enriching its prior brief grouped-by-RLS-posture list); the "Total System" connector table → dropped outright, confirmed fully redundant with `ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md`'s own canonical connector table (every row — OpenMontage/OpenArt/fal.ai/Graphify CC/FFmpeg/Whisper/Supabase/Anthropic — already present there in more detail before this move). Every cross-reference to `manual.html` across CLAUDE.md/`interconnection_map.md`/`ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md`/the `.claude/skills/*.md` files repointed in the same pass.
- **minotaur_map.html** — **rebuilt Sep 23 2026, real Alex-confirmed scope change, then recalibrated the same day on his own direct follow-up feedback ("too simple... doesn't give full picture of what rpgace is, the domains and ai used and functions"), then absorbed `manual.html`'s retirement as the SOLE remaining Tier (a) explaining doc alongside patch_notes.html.** No longer a 1:1 translation of `system_flow_map.md`'s Mermaid diagrams (its original July 18 design, which had drifted into naming real Supabase tables/module/function identifiers and a full internal dev-tooling section — genuinely "revealing how the system works," the opposite of its own consumer-facing job) — but the FIRST simplification pass over-corrected, collapsing to 6 abstract "journeys" with almost no real domain/feature substance. **Real, current shape**: organized by the 9 real domains (Oracle, the Knowledge Tree, Bookworm, Content & Video Pipeline, Cooking, Shopping Wishlist, Schedule & Journal, Chronicles, Quest Board), each with a real plain-language description of its actual functions (recipe generation, director-style blending, chapter-by-chapter book reading, budget-aware wishlist fitting, etc.), a real one-scenario worked-example walkthrough per domain (Minotaur/Manual Unification M4 — a concrete end-to-end sequence in plain language, zero code/table names), and a dedicated "The AI Itself" section naming Claude (Anthropic) as the real underlying AI and explaining, in plain terms, why RPGACE uses more than one model tier (judgment work vs. research/outlining vs. mechanical cleanup). Still zero code identifiers, table names, or internal architecture anywhere on the page — real functions and real domain names are not internal implementation detail, they're what the product actually does. All genuinely internal/developer-facing detail (the former "Total System Gateway" connector cluster, the "Oversight Rivers" self-referential section, and now `manual.html`'s own former button catalog/table reference) lives only in the Galaxy Map and Tier (b)'s truth docs, never duplicated back onto this page. Update when a real domain or function is added/changes shape — an internal implementation change underneath an existing function does not need a new page here.

### Tier (b) — "Truth docs," what's actually real, verified against each other and against live code/Supabase
- **interconnection_map.md** — structural touchpoints between modules; Oracle and Taxonomy Tree are the two real hubs everything converges on except Schedule (fully independent).
- **system_flow_map.md** — Mermaid yes/no flow diagrams of every pipeline (information pulled → processed → transported → output, with merges shown), planned-feature attach points, and the built/not-built truth table — the same underlying data as `interconnection_map.md`, grouped into the bigger "rivers" `minotaur_map.html` draws from. **Update this whenever architecture changes, alongside the others.**
- **ai_tooling_and_rules_map.md** — July 31: catalogs every Claude Code skill actually present in this environment (global/native vs. RPGACE-authored, and what each one really does to this system) plus every rule-bearing `.md`/`.txt` file in the repo, sorted into live authority vs. historical record, AND what exists external to RPGACE that no Claude Code session can change code-wise (OpenMontage, OpenArt, Composio). Update in the same session a new global skill gets used against RPGACE, a new RPGACE skill ships, or a new rule file is added anywhere.
- **perspective_map.html — Tier (b)'s 4th doc, new Sep 15 2026, real Alex ask run through a full `/paranoia` pass.** A live-query page (`taxonomy_map.html`'s own real anon-key-fetch pattern, reused not reinvented) reading `perspective_reports` directly on every load — every real element (galaxy/node/l0_unit/river-synthesis/module/level-synthesis/dimension_kind-synthesis) shown grouped by scope, each report's own real cross-references rendered as clickable links to whichever OTHER loaded report shares that relationship (an unresolvable reference stays plain text, never a dead link) — the actual "relative to each other" mechanism Alex asked for. Deliberately separates the real evidence-checked reports from the 233 `mainjs_function`/`onclick_feature`/`decision` scaffold rows (100% `status='unverified'`, auto-generated, never evidence-checked) into their own clearly-labeled, collapsed section — a truth-documentation doc cannot show stale scaffold as equivalent to real evidence without undermining its own purpose. Real division of labor, Alex's own confirmed answer: this doc is the one full cross-referenced picture; a Galaxy Map page's own inline report snippet (queued, `GMP-A`) is that one element's local context, linking out here for the full relative view — both coexist, distinct jobs. Verified via headless Chromium with the Supabase boundary mocked (real network to Supabase is blocked from this dev environment): cross-reference resolution works correctly (a resolvable relationship renders as a real link; an unresolvable one stays honest plain text), the scope-grouping and scaffold-separation both render correctly, zero console errors. **Real, closed Sep 16 2026**: `pg_policies` confirmed `perspective_reports` genuinely carries `anon_read_only` (SELECT, `qual:true`, role `anon`) — the live page's own anon-key fetch will work, not just assumed. Both `perspective_map.html` (commit `31a4763`) and the CLAUDE.md logging commit (`ea8c7b8`) confirmed `state: READY` on the real Vercel production deployment via `list_deployments`. Full record: `records/2026-09/perspective_oversight_doc_paranoia_2026-09-15.txt`. **Still genuinely open: no browser has actually loaded the page — headless-mocked and RLS-confirmed, not hand-tested.**
- **Real Sep 15→16 continuation: "Perspective Full Coverage" plan complete — all 58 real registered modules and all 12 live rivers now have a real evidence-grounded `perspective_reports` row, plus 6 more covering L0-L3 and the infra/inter Dimension kinds.** The Supabase MCP connector went down for a real, multi-hour outage right after `perspective_map.html` shipped — 26 queued module+river reports (14 modules, 12 river syntheses, each a genuine first-person self-report reconstructed from real code evidence, never invented) plus 6 more (4 level syntheses, 2 dimension_kind syntheses) sat drafted-but-unwritten until reconnect, then landed in one pass once Supabase came back. Matching `ceo_plan_items` rows (26 of them, `MOD-<name>`/`RIVER-<n>` item codes) flipped green with real evidence (`perspective_report_id`) linking each plan item to the report that satisfies it. **Real follow-up plan created, not yet executed**: "Galaxy Map Structure Based on Perspective" (`ceo_plans`, `basis='ratified'`, depends on the now-complete coverage plan) — 3 bounded items, all still red (build not started, per Alex's own "wait for the 26 to land first, then create the tracking rows, don't build in the same pass" sequencing): **GMP-A** bakes each river/module/L0-unit's report inline into its own Galaxy Map page; **GMP-B** is a consistency-CHECK (not a regeneration) comparing `RIVER_FLOWS`' claimed edges against the reports' own `cross_refs`, flagging real mismatches to `system_map_flags`; **GMP-C** relabels L0 into 3 explicit tiers (galaxy/node/l0_unit), cosmetic/additive only. All 3 are exempt from the standing Galaxy-Map-pause in the same way the Orchestrator/OpenMontage split was — closing a gap in already-perspective'd work, not adding new surface area — but still queued behind the product-debt/verification-debt backlogs named in the pause bullet, not started this pass. The "Logic dimension" half of Alex's original ask was dropped outright on his own direct "Nevermind" once the standalone Logic Dimension page's Sep 1 2026 retirement (G111) was surfaced to him. **Tense discipline, Aug 11 (same day, later pass) — Alex's own explicit framing, now the real organizing rule across the whole tier system**: Tier (a)/(b) plus layers (c)/(d) are deliberately **present tense only** — only 🟢 green (verified built and live) content ever lands there, so what they describe is always what's actually true right now. Layer (e) below is deliberately **past tense and future tense** — planned-but-unbuilt, mid-build, and (once built) archived-superseded content — so a reader can always tell, from which artifact something appears in, whether it's calibrated-present, not-yet-real, or no-longer-current, without reading the prose itself to find out.

### Historical session state — archived July 31

The dated "Current state (July 24)" and "Prior state (July 18 / 20 / 22 / 23)" sections that used to sit here have been moved verbatim to **`CLAUDE_archive.md`**, per this file's own doc-discipline rule. They were ~10,000 words of session-by-session narrative, almost all of it either superseded by the Current State section above, already told in full in `patch_notes.html`, or duplicated in the Security note and Known landmines sections below.

Every fact in them that is still load-bearing has been hoisted into a permanent section of this file (Current State, Known landmines, Security note, or Invokable frameworks). Read the archive only for archaeology — never for orientation.

### Building guide for lower models — follow these patterns exactly
1. **New feature = new module** in rpgace_core.js: `RPGACE.register('name', {...})` between markers; `init()` wires listeners/injection with `setTimeout` delays (existing modules use 1300-1700ms) plus a `page:show` hook to re-inject on navigation.
2. **Supabase reads**: `RPGACE.sb.select(table, params)` (returns parsed JSON). **Writes needing the new row back**: raw `fetch` + `Prefer: return=representation` + `response.ok` check. Simple fire-and-forget writes: `RPGACE.sb.insert/update/del`.
3. **Oracle calls from LEARNING features**: reuse `phylumPath._callExtractor` / `_callGroundWorkerJSON` / `_callGroundWorkerText` — never hand-roll another fetch to `/api/oracle`. Keep max_tokens ≤1000 and ask for bounded word counts (the 504 bug scales with response length: 700 tokens worked, 1200 truncated, 1800 failed outright).
4. **Every taxonomy write gets a human checkpoint** before committing — either an inline confirm popup (patterns: `_showPlacementConfirm`, `_showArticleConfirm`) or staging through `taxonomy_proposals` → review queue. No exceptions; this rule has caught real garbage twice.
5. **Never trust model JSON blindly**: parse defensively, and sanitize structure-affecting output in code (pattern: `_sanitizeNewSteps` — strips path-like or duplicate step names so a prompt regression can't corrupt the tree even if it slips past the prompt wording).
6. **Long AI jobs stream**: await only the first result, continue the rest in background appending to Supabase, poll from the UI (pattern: `_analyzeChapter` + `_continueAnalyzingInBackground` + `analysis_complete` flag). Never make the user wait minutes for a full batch — a 13-insight chapter used to take ~7 minutes before anything appeared.
7. **Fail loud**: surface errors as toasts; never silently swallow a failed write or fake success/progress. Bookworm's first hand-test proved why.
8. **Confirm destructive actions**: two-click arm/confirm (pattern: Bookworm's 🗑, 3s timeout) — no heavy modal needed.
9. **Chat triggers**: chainable `window.sendChat` wrap with a `TRIGGER_PREFIXES` array and a `window._xPatched` guard flag (patterns: scheduleOracle, bookworm). Wraps must fall through to the original for non-matching input.
10. **Model IDs**: ground worker is always `claude-sonnet-4-6`; purely MECHANICAL text jobs (formatting cleanup, one-line rewording — no judgment) may use `phylumPath.MECHANICAL_MODEL` (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, added July 19 with explicit confirmation). Never `claude-sonnet-4-20250514`, never `claude-3-5-sonnet`, never guess a new identifier.
11. **Token cost is a design constraint** (July 19, after £10 burned in one test session — full audit: `token_cost_audit_2026-07-19.txt`): never send full slash-joined path listings in prompts (use numbered indented name trees); prefilter candidate lists with the free keyword scan before any Oracle call; skip the call outright when the prefilter finds nothing; never add a second premium-model "triage" call in front of a ground-worker call.
12. **Large variable content going to Oracle routes through `phylumPath._condenseIfLarge(text, context)` first, never a bare `.slice(0, N)` truncation** (Aug 11, real bug found: Bookworm's `_analyzeChapter()` was silently dropping any chapter content past 12000 raw chars, no marker, no log). The helper short-circuits at zero cost under 6000 chars, strips genuine filler via the cheap `MECHANICAL_MODEL` tier while preserving every specific technique/quote/attribution exactly, and fails open to the untouched original text on any error — a real truncation cap applied AFTER condensing is honest (holds more real content, doesn't remove the safety limit), a bare truncation applied to raw uncondensed text is a silent content-loss bug waiting to happen.
13. **When writing `oracleAppGrounding.SELF_KNOWLEDGE` (rpgace_core.js), name the real owning module or Supabase table explicitly whenever stating what a feature does or doesn't do — never describe it only by bare function name** (Sep 23 2026, real rule earned by the Quest Board bug: the original wrong claim named only `addXP()`/`completeQuest()`, no module, which is exactly why `scripts/check_self_knowledge_contradictions.py` — the standing content-correctness check, see the matching session-start-check section — could not have caught it at the time; confirmed by a real regression test replaying the actual old text). An unanchored claim about a feature is invisible to that mechanism; a claim anchored to a real `/* ===MODULE:name=== */` identifier or a real Supabase table name is checkable, and stays checkable for as long as that identifier exists.
